===== BEGIN SOURCE 01 | AGENTS.md | sha256=dbd09563760bc378bbe2bc7ace7689fe69a94d8b94a0e996cfc709c9b5beed1d =====
# Moonwalking Agent Workflow

These rules apply to every agent working in this repository.

## Canonical repository

- Use the active Moonwalking Git checkout connected to the project's authoritative remote. Its local path may differ between computers and does not need to be inside OneDrive.
- Confirm the checkout with `git remote -v`, `git status --short --branch`, and `git worktree list` before making changes.
- For local-only work, keep both the checkout and its Git common directory outside OneDrive or other synced folders. Verify with `git rev-parse --show-toplevel --absolute-git-dir --git-common-dir`. A worktree outside OneDrive is insufficient if its Git storage still points into OneDrive.
- Do not use `DEVv\recovered\Moonwalking` for development, merging, or history repair.
- Treat committed Git history as the source of truth. Imported chat history provides context but does not override the repository state.
- For another-computer and master-PC access procedures, read `docs/ai/WORK_FROM_ANYWHERE.md`.

## Before changing files

1. Run `git fetch --all --prune`.
2. Inspect `git status --short --branch`, recent `git log`, `git worktree list`, and the current diff.
3. Read `docs/ai/AI_INDEX.md`, `HANDOFF.md`, and `MW_BACKLOG.md`.
4. Check whether another agent has active or uncommitted work that overlaps the requested files.

## Protect active work

- Do not switch branches, merge, rebase, reset, restore, clean, or discard changes until every uncommitted file has been identified and preserved.
- Do not edit another agent's active worktree.
- Use a separate named branch and worktree for parallel Claude and Codex work.
- Resolve overlapping work through commits and reviewed diffs rather than copying files between active worktrees.

## Handoff requirements

Before handing work to another agent or declaring it complete:

1. Run the relevant tests and checks.
2. Commit coherent changes with a descriptive message.
3. Push the branch unless the user explicitly requests a local-only checkpoint.
4. Report the branch, commit, checks run, and any remaining modified or untracked files.
5. For the instant-radar lane, update `docs/ai/RESUME_HERE.md` and
   `docs/ai/ACTIVE_WORK.json` whenever the active branch, implementation
   checkpoint, review ownership/status, evidence location, gates or next
   action changes. Push the checkpoint with the work. Keep code-commit
   references distinct from later documentation-only commits, preserve other
   agents' status unless verified, and carry a pointer forward if the active
   branch changes. Another computer/session must be able to resume from GitHub.

===== END SOURCE 01 =====

# AI Index

Project: BHABIT Moonwalking

Current instant-radar continuation: [Resume from anywhere](RESUME_HERE.md)
and [active work record](ACTIVE_WORK.json). Check these before relying on
an older chat or MacBook handoff for the current research branch/state.

Local no-credit helper: `scripts/local_ai.py`

- Usage and safety: `docs/ai/LOCAL_ASSISTANT.md`
- Good for grounded project questions, diff review, and log triage.
- Do not use the local 9B model as an unsupervised repository agent.

Read in this order before implementation:

1. `MW_SPEC.md`
2. `MW_BACKLOG.md`
3. `ARCHITECTURE.md`
4. `HANDOFF.md`
5. The relevant files under `docs/`
6. Touching CSS, colors, spacing, typography, or anything visual on any
   page: `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` first. It's the cross-system token
   reference (values live in `frontend/src/styles/design-tokens.css`) and
   says where the home board and the Moonwalking `/app/*` shell already
   agree, where they genuinely still disagree on purpose, and known gaps
   flagged but not silently fixed. `docs/FRONTEND_UI_RULES.md` and
   `docs/UI_CONTRACT.md` remain authoritative for the home board's own
   grid/row/motion rules specifically.

## Canonical runtime

- Frontend: Vite on `127.0.0.1:5173`
- Board API: Flask on `127.0.0.1:5003`
- Real-source sentiment: FastAPI on `127.0.0.1:8003`, accessed by the browser through Flask
- Local start: `./start_app.sh`
- Board endpoint: `GET /data`
- Health: `GET /api/health`

There is no active bridge service. Ports 5001, 5002, 5100, and 8002 are legacy.

On Tom's Windows PC, port 5003 is the **always-on collector**, not a dev
server: a scheduled task runs it from the `Moonwalking-Runtime` worktree and
writes every store to `%USERPROFILE%\.moonwalking\data`. Don't kill it or use
`--kill-port` on 5003; run dev backends on another port. See
`docs/developer/ALWAYS_ON_COLLECTOR.md`.

## Active frontend

- Mount: `frontend/src/main.jsx`
- Root router: `frontend/src/App.jsx`
- Public board: `frontend/src/components/DashboardShell.jsx`
- Single board poller: `frontend/src/context/DataContext.jsx`
- Guest/auth watchlists: `frontend/src/context/WatchlistContext.jsx`
- Product routes: `frontend/src/mvp/MvpApp.jsx`

Do not treat `frontend/src/Dashboard.jsx`, `legacy913`, playgrounds, backups, or original files as the active board.

## Active backend

- Board API and workers: `backend/app.py`
- Sentiment service: `backend/sentiment_api.py`
- Watchlist/auth blueprint: `backend/watchlist.py`
- Coin context: `backend/coin_intel_external.py`
- Local coin pressure: `backend/insights.py`
- Price baseline persistence: `backend/price_db.py`
- Canonical alerts: `backend/alerts_engine.py`

## Active alert/sentiment docs

- Alert behavior and thresholds: `docs/user-guides/ALERTS_USE_GUIDE.md`
- Alert contract: `docs/alerts_engine_spec.md`
- Sentiment/source matrix: `docs/SENTIMENT_SOURCES.md`
- Current audit: `docs/MOONWALKINGS_APP_AUDIT_2026-07-14.md`
- Handoff state: `HANDOFF.md`

## Non-negotiable data rules

- Real data only. Missing data is null/empty plus warming, unavailable, stale, or offline state.
- Canonical identity is `product_id`; UI text is the base symbol.
- Exactly one board polling context and one backend worker set.
- 1h price uses a real 1h price baseline.
- 1h volume uses a real 1h volume baseline. Never infer it from price.
- Market-wide sentiment must not be described as coin-specific.
- The frontend must not probe random ports or persist a noncanonical backend.

## UI rules

- One continuous board wrapper.
- Use the existing row and panel primitives; do not add a second layout system.
- Keep action controls stable. Animate data cells, not the whole row hit target.
- Top order: 1h price, 1m movers, 3m gainers/losers, Intelligence Log, Watchlist, 1h volume.
- Use Raleway and the current design tokens.

## Verification

```bash
npm run guardrails
.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q backend/tests
(cd frontend && npm run verify)
bash scripts/smoke_check.sh
bash scripts/smoke_sentiment_proxy.sh BTC
```

Do not commit or push unless Tom explicitly asks.

The user wants the market-signal work prioritized and deep, independent reviews

## Verified starting point

- Source branch: `codex/instant-radar-disconnect-diagnosis-20261111`, `f291b35`.
- New Codex branch: `codex/instant-radar-research-restart-20261113`.
- Reader runtime remains `cfa4aff`; latest offline verification is `975234e`.

  versus a +0.02 requirement, block interval included zero, and the primary

# Instant-radar v2 review acceptance — 2026-10-30

Codex reviewed Claude's pushed `10b0f96`, reproduced its catalog defect before
applying the patch, and cherry-picked the review, probes and fix as `1da6f4e` on
`codex/instant-radar-20261028`. Black 24.8.0 formatting follows at **`92fb51d`**,
the current implementation. Parsed code is equivalent to Claude's patch apart
from docstring whitespace. Claude's worktree and earlier branch are untouched.

Read [Claude's original review](reviews/CLAUDE_INSTANT_V2_REVIEW_20261030.md)
and [Codex's verification record](results/instant_v2_review_acceptance_20261030/CHECKS.json).

## Accepted fix

Before the patch, `forward_event_catalog` stamped a verified v1 label as a v2
forward window although `forward_alert_timing` rejected it. The wrapper now
requires the v2 draft. The generic v1 catalog and target formulas are unchanged.
Codex verified rejection of v1 input and acceptance of genuine v2 and empty
generator inputs. This corrects provenance/API consistency; it supplies no
market-performance evidence.

Both contract JSON files are byte-identical to `8b5b266`. The source edit and
formatting change the evaluator hash. Regenerate unfrozen v2 labels with the
accepted source before new evaluation; do not mix evaluator hashes. No study
labels were frozen here. The old Gemini packet and reproduction record remain
pinned to the initial `8b5b266` source; their hash is not the current hash. The
new verification record contains current-source examples. Full proposal
fidelity still requires the unamended original.

## Decisions before study freeze

| Item | Codex disposition |
|---|---|
| S1: earlier wick controls strict early/late timing | Reproduced: a wick ten seconds before the trigger gives `late_after_activity_trigger` with about 8.9% consumed at the trigger. This follows the whole-reference-window peak rule. Retaining it, changing the peak reference, or requiring a sustained extreme needs an explicit prespecified choice. No policy changed here. |
| S2: sigma and pace cutoffs differ | Confirmed causal: sigma ends at the completed minute before anchor minus 35 seconds; pace ends at anchor minus 30 seconds. They estimate different quantities. This is not future-data leakage. Decide whether to retain the distinction and verify the recorder's pace construction. |
| S3: null fraction lacks a reason | Independently reproduced for a complete forward-only label with no positive peak relative to anchor minus 30 seconds. Record an explicit non-positive-reference-peak reason before freezing the reporting schema. No field/contract change was silently added here. |

The target code review is complete with one accepted fix. The study is **not
frozen or ready for fitting**. Gemini's v2 consistency review, physical-origin
or explicit proxy policy, association/exposure and operational denominators,
exact F1-F5 formulas, stages/readiness, feed gate and recorder contract remain

The "all tags must be explicit" doc wording is corrected. |
| Health at each cutoff | To verify every 5s cutoff, sufficiently granular spans must be completed and their evidence available by that cutoff. Late verdicts remain late; adapters must preserve cutoff, publication and availability times. |
| Trusted assertions | Shape/known-at checks do not independently prove subscriptions, trade coverage or quote quality. Feed gate remains false. |
| Recorder/source identity | Baselines now retain producer hash and full replay provenance: reader hash, producer/reader equality and canonical input-manifest hash. Reopen can pin `expected_recorder_sha256`; mismatches are refused. Default historical reading remains possible with a visible producer mismatch, rather than pretending a new reader produced old data. Metadata checks do not authenticate inputs or replace frozen raw-artifact manifests. |
| Two missing-data causes | Added `pre_window_reference_unavailable_reason` and `publication_unavailable_reason`. Original reason precedence is retained; both independent causes are visible without changing target labels. |

beta, flat-benchmark fallback and the 120-window pace medians. Later conflict,

# Bounded reconciliation accepted with continuity barriers

November 4, 2026 (Pacific). Codex verified the pushed
`claude/instant-coverage-reconciliation` tip `1af0a64`, reviewed the three
commits separately and integrated them on `codex/instant-radar-20261028`:

|---|---|---|

| `d26c57d` | `d80418e` | Per-product continuity epochs and barrier tests |
| `1af0a64` | `055d590` | Second 30-case barrier probe set |

The original [boundary blocker](RECONCILIATION_BOUNDARY_REVIEW_20261104.md)
is resolved. In isolated temporary source trees, Codex reproduced **26 failures
and four passing controls** from the second probe set on unmodified `bfab9c8`;
all **30 pass** on `1af0a64`. This includes the original same-millisecond audit
reset counterexample. The local Claude worktree was inspected read-only and
left untouched. The user reported two Claude sessions writing the review
branch; the commits were reviewed individually, with no force push or copying
of active worktree files.

## Accepted behavior

The first check remains exact receipt-interval trade-ID equality. Only the
two ordering-failure classes can be reconciled, within **four proofs,
10,000ms and 100,000 IDs**. Original failures remain on record; a later
merged proof supersedes them only once its computation has completed.

Each product has a continuity epoch. Connection changes, audit/transport/
queue failures and lost or re-added required subscriptions advance it in
receipt-sequence order. Merging, assessment chaining and interval trade
counts cannot cross an epoch, including at one shared millisecond with a
reused connection ID. Healthy repeated acknowledgements preserve continuity;
valid intervals and races on either side of a barrier remain usable separately.
Trades keep their first receipt and are never shifted into another bucket.

Merges are deliberately disjoint. A nested race around an already merged
pair can still cause a false veto. The caps were chosen after observing one
hour's coverage, without outcome analysis; they remain engineering limits
in a draft amendment. Neither those caps nor this review freeze the study.

The feed verdict is unchanged, V1 stays default, physical onset is undefined,

===== BEGIN SOURCE 13 | docs/research/reviews/CLAUDE_INSTANT_V2_REVIEW_20261030.md | sha256=739c6be664149fa56af4493d0063e58bafe7188313310e289015a1acf15fd667 =====
# Claude independent review of instant-target v2 draft — 2026-10-30

Reviewed implementation `8b5b266` (`codex/instant-radar-20261028`; `scripts/` and `tests/` byte-identical at tip `5e02268`).
Branch `claude/instant-radar-v2-review`. Synthetic probes only: no fitting, no historical discovery/validation, no live,
recorder or deployment change. Probes: `tests/test_instant_targets_v2_review.py`.

**Verdict: v2 targets are ready for protocol freeze, subject to the study decisions below.** One low-severity reproduced
defect was found and fixed. No defect was found in the labels themselves. The remaining blockers are study definitions and
the gates already listed in the contract, not code errors.

## Reproduced defect (fixed here)

**R1 — low. `forward_event_catalog` accepted v1 labels** (`instant_targets.py:forward_event_catalog`).
`forward_alert_timing` refuses v1 labels, but the forward catalog accepted them and stamped
`catalog_kind="forward_window_not_physical_impulse"`, a v2-only semantic, onto v1 rows without an `evaluator_sha256`.
* Reproduction: v1 `TargetEvaluator(samples with trade_count=3).evaluate(...)` → `verified_forward=True`;
  `forward_event_catalog([label])` returned one entry; `forward_alert_timing` raised.
* Expected: refuse, like the timing API. Actual: accepted.
* Fix: 3 lines; `forward_event_catalog` requires `contract_version == "instant-target-v2-draft"`. The generic
  `event_catalog` and all v1 behaviour are unchanged. Regression: `test_forward_catalog_refuses_v1_labels_like_forward_timing_does`.
* Effect: the edit changes `evaluator_sha256`, so any labels generated at `8b5b266` must be regenerated. None are frozen.

## Confirmed correct (with independent evidence)

| Area | Evidence |
|---|---|
| v1 valid-label behaviour unchanged | `test_v1_outputs_identical_to_pre_v2_reference`: the current code under the pinned v1 contract gives **dict-identical** `evaluate` output to the pre-v2 implementation `cb7034a`. Coverage: 40 random paths × up/down × 300/900 s, plus `alert_timing` at four publication times for every verified onset. |
| v2 delta is only the cutoff and named additions | `test_v2_differs_from_v1_only_by_cutoff_and_added_keys`: removing the nine named v2 keys gives v2 output equal to v1 output on the same rows (40 paths × directions × horizons). No other field changed. |
| Baseline cutoff (adaptation of `d711832`) | Same rule as `d711832` (`floor((anchor-35s)/60s)*60s`), applied only when `is_v2`, and it matches the contract's `sigma_end_rule`. `test_v2_rejects_the_v1_cutoff_when_they_differ`: at offsets 0, 5, 30, 34 s past the minute, v2 rejects the v1 end as misaligned; at 35, 36 and 55 s the two ends coincide. |
| Pre-window vs post-anchor peaks | Log additivity holds: `residual(ref,ts) = residual(ref,anchor) + residual(anchor,ts)`, so the pre-window peak equals the forward peak (same timestamp, values within 1e-12) unless an earlier point is higher. 60 random paths × both directions. |
| Peak search including earlier wicks | A wick inside `[anchor-30s, anchor)` becomes `pre_window_peak_ts_ms` (< anchor); `peak_ts_ms` stays ≥ anchor. Both are correct per contract; see S1. |
| Both directions | `test_up_and_down_labels_mirror`: residual paths built with opposite signs give identical status, flags, peak/breakout timestamps and residual values (within 1e-9) for up and down. 30 paths. |
| Maturity | `asof = end-1` → `pending`; `asof = end` → `ready`. The pre-window reference never extends maturity. |
| Missingness never False | A gap at `anchor-35s` keeps the forward label and the pre-window reference `ready`, but sets onset `unavailable` and `verified_onset=None`. A `last_trade` price source inside the pre-window gives `pre_window_reference_status=unavailable/price_fallback`, onset `unavailable`, and the forward label `ready`. |
| Backward publication lookup | Publications at grid+0 and grid+4999 ms both use the grid point at or before them, never the next one. Quote age is measured from the publication: 6.0 s passes; 10.999 s is `stale_alert_quote`. |
| Mixed-provenance rejection | v1 and v2 labels mixed in one catalog are rejected (contract hash). A v2 label with its `evaluator_sha256` removed, mixed with a genuine one, is rejected (evaluator set). Forged labels are rejected by both timing APIs (existing test). |

## Unresolved study definitions (decide before freeze; not code defects)

**S1 — earlier wick inside the activity window makes strict "early" unattainable.**
`_v2_alert_timing` requires `publish_ts_ms <= pre_window_peak_ts_ms`. If the highest point is a wick in
`[anchor-30s, anchor)`, every post-trigger publication is `late_after_activity_trigger`, even when
`pre_window_consumed_fraction` is below 0.5.
* Reproduction: `test_earlier_wick_makes_every_strict_publication_late`. A one-point 4.5% wick at `anchor-10s`, then a +2%
  forward leg with no retrace, is a verified strict onset. At the anchor the fraction is about 0.09 but the timing is
  `late`, with a negative `lead_to_pre_window_peak_seconds`.
* The code conforms to the contract (`pre_window_peak_search: reference..anchor+H`). Decide one of:
  (a) keep it: a consumed wick counts as already priced;
  (b) use the post-anchor peak for the "before peak" clause and the pre-window peak only for the fraction;
  (c) require a quote-validated, multi-point extreme.
  A one-grid-point wick is also exposed to quote noise.

**S2 — asymmetric exclusion windows.** The sigma baseline ends before `anchor-35s`, but the pace baseline ends at
`anchor-30s` (`pace_end_ts_ms == anchor - velocity_ms`). So the previous velocity window's first 5 s can still inform
pace. That is causal and not leakage, but decide whether the two should share a cutoff. Recorder provenance for the
120-window pace builder is still unverified (as the triage already notes).

**S3 — non-positive pre-window peak has no reason code.** For a forward-only label whose path from `anchor-30s` never rises
above it, `pre_window_consumed_fraction` is `None` with no reason. Strict timing cannot reach this case, because a
verified onset implies a positive run-up. Decide whether `None` should carry a reason
(e.g. `non_positive_pre_window_peak`) in the forward API's output.

**S4 — still open from the contract.** Physical-origin policy, event association/exposure denominators, the exact F1–F5
formulas, live-stage thresholds, readiness minima and the feed gate. Full fidelity to the original proposal still needs the
unamended original.

## Optional suggestions

* Record `contract_version` in the catalog outputs, and check for a single `product_id` per `evaluator_sha256` group if
  catalogs ever pool evaluators from mixed sources.
* `label` fields used by the timing APIs (`beta`, `pre_window_*`) are trusted after the hash check. Recomputing them from
  rows would remove reliance on an unmodified label dict.

* `bash scripts/validate_mw_guardrails.sh`: all guardrails passed.

  Frozen historical hashes were not recomputed; no historical artifact was touched.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 14 | docs/research/reviews/CLAUDE_INSTANT_OFFLINE_REVIEW_20261030.md | sha256=2122d60d50ec25f8ce7de76af584f3d7b9809d773f7c7b644cbc657b5b788721 =====
# Claude independent review of the offline instant pipeline — 2026-10-30

Reviewed implementation `8211262` (`codex/instant-radar-20261028`); prompt `docs/ai/INSTANT_OFFLINE_REVIEW_PROMPT_20261030.md`.
Branch `claude/instant-offline-review`. Synthetic probes only (`tests/test_micro_replay_review.py`): no capture, collector
connection, fitting, historical rerun, feed-gate change or live change. The accepted target code was not re-reviewed.

**Verdict:** the offline replay, baseline builder and diagnostics behave as their draft contracts state, with one
low-severity recorder defect, which is fixed here. The baseline matches an independent numpy computation. No probe made a
later quote, recovery, conflict or coverage verdict alter an earlier feature.

## Reproduced defect (fixed)

**D1 — low. A monotonic-clock regression passed when the packet in between had no monotonic time**
(`micro_replay.py: ReplayStore._ingest`).
* Reproduction: packets carrying `receipt_monotonic_ns` 100 s, then none, then 50 s, with increasing receipt times.
  The third packet was accepted.
* Expected: abort and mark the artifact `failed`, like any other clock regression. Actual: it was accepted, because the
  check compared only with the immediately previous packet's `mono`, which was null.
* Fix: compare with the last packet that carried a monotonic time. Regression:
  `test_monotonic_regression_across_a_packet_without_monotonic_time_fails_closed`.
* Real feed-audit envelopes always carry `receipt_monotonic_ns`, so captured data is unlikely to hit this. The fix changes
  `recorder_sha256`; no replay artifact is frozen.

## Examined, not a defect

**Untagged `source` defaults to `ws`.** The pipeline doc says source tags "must be explicit and recognized", but
`coinbase_feed_audit.envelope()` writes no `source` field. The default is therefore required to read real WebSocket
captures. REST trades live in a separate file in a different shape (no `type`), so they cannot become executions even when
untagged. Unknown tags fail closed. Only a REST row re-wrapped as a WebSocket `match` message would be at risk.
**Action: correct the doc wording**, not the code. Pinned by
`test_untagged_source_defaults_to_ws_but_rest_shaped_trades_still_never_count`.

## Verified behaviour

| Area | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Baseline: 61 closes, 60 returns, sample SD | Constructed 2-hour history (anchor 120 min + 15 s): `sigma_end` 7,140,000, `pace_end` 7,185,000, 60 returns. `sigma_1m == np.std(returns, ddof=1)` (rel 1e-9). |
| Beta and fallback | `beta == np.polyfit(btc, coin, 1)[0]` (rel 1e-9). Flat BTC → beta 1 with `beta_fallback=True`. Missing BTC history → `unavailable/baseline_coverage` for `BTC-USD`, never a fallback. |
| 120 non-overlapping pace windows | Medians of trade count and USD equal numpy medians of the constructed per-window values. A trade received exactly at `pace_end` is excluded (half-open windows). All availability fields equal the anchor. |
| Later knowledge cannot reach back | A conflicting duplicate known at 113 s, a negative health audit known at 120 s and a quote received after the cutoff all leave the cutoff-105 s snapshot byte-identical. Explicit later `asof` queries do see the conflict and the audit. A span known only after the query's as-of does not verify it. |
| Receipt boundaries and boundary count | Read and covered by the existing suite: `[start, end)` receipts, `trade_count_at_window_start` = receipts exactly at the start, matching the target's bucket-start exclusion. |
| Dedup, sides, exclusions | First receipt wins across connections; a conflicting duplicate adds an issue known at its own receipt. Maker side is inverted for taker amounts. Unknown-side USD is retained and makes signed flow unavailable. `last_match`, heartbeats and REST/recovery add no executions. |
| Quotes and sparse zero vs outage | Original quote receipt time kept; > 10 s stale, invalid, missing or crossing an unverified interval → unavailable midpoint. Verified empty buckets read as zero; unverified → `None`, never zero. |
| Read-only and non-overwrite | `create` uses exclusive open (existing paths refused). Reopen is SQLite read-only. Only `complete` artifacts are queryable. Failed or sealed artifacts cannot be written. |
| Diagnostics do not alter labels | `alert_report` builds a new dict from the unchanged timing result and never writes into `label`. The whole-window and forward-peak fractions carry separate names and reasons. `forward_catalog_report` keeps catalog selection and only annotates left-censoring. |

## Study and integration items (not code defects)

1. **Live coverage needs completed spans per cutoff.** Health spans count only once `available >= end`. So a snapshot at
   cutoff `c` is verified only by spans that end at or before `c` and are known by `c`. A live health monitor must emit
   completed spans at least every 5 s bucket, or every live snapshot reads `unverified_coverage`. The adapter and monitor
   contract should state this explicitly.
2. **Health assertions are trusted inputs.** The store checks shape and known-at times, not truth. Independent
   subscription and reconciliation evidence is still required (as the doc says). The feed gate stays false.
3. **The recorder hash is recorded but not checked on reopen.** `meta.recorder_sha256` is written but never compared on
   read-only open, and the baseline provenance records only the replay contract hash. Optional: record the recorder hash
   in `build_baseline` provenance so a replay built by older code is visible.
4. **Diagnostic reason precedence (optional).** When both the pre-window reference and the publication are unavailable,
   `alert_report` reports the pre-window reason (`whole_reason or timing reason`). The publication's own reason (for
   example `stale_alert_quote`) is then only in the timing fields. Consider reporting both.

  unchecked.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 15 | docs/research/reviews/CLAUDE_INSTANT_COVERAGE_REVIEW_20261103.md | sha256=bc5f318b286d791e0df78d7e2b63a86f839a053adfc28f2f32465b6ea7601255 =====
# Claude independent review of the coverage monitor and paired samples — 2026-11-03

Reviewed implementation `4ed9045` (`codex/instant-radar-20261028`; code identical at tip `6c22952`): `instant_coverage.py`,
`instant_samples.py`, their two draft contracts, the policy document and `tests/test_instant_coverage.py`.
Branch `claude/instant-coverage-review`. Probes: `tests/test_instant_coverage_review.py`.

No fitting, new capture, live hook, outcome evaluation, or historical discovery/validation. The preserved one-hour capture
was replayed read-only to check real envelope shapes and to classify failed proofs. Trade IDs, receipt times and trade
counts were read; no price, return or label was analysed. Output stayed outside Git and the capture's folder.

**Verdict: sound as an offline, fail-closed ID check, with three small defects fixed here. It should not become target
coverage until D1 is decided.** On real data its strict rule produced only false vetoes, and those would remove most
labels. D1 is a contract decision, so I measured it and did not change it.

## D1 — highest priority: strict interval attribution vetoes real coverage (design decision, measured)

`_ingest` requires each heartbeat pair's expected ID set to equal the IDs received in that half-open receipt interval. A
heartbeat that overtakes its match on the wire fails two adjacent intervals, although nothing was lost.

Replay of `feed_audit_campaign_20261028/attempt-01/websocket.jsonl.gz` (63,258 packets, input hash `4b4a56ec…`):

| Product | Proofs | Failed | Expected IDs | Never received | 5s buckets vetoed |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| BTC-USD | 3,599 | 29 | 21,770 | **0** | 16 / 719 (2.2%) |
| SOL-USD | 3,599 | 4 | 2,641 | **0** | 2 / 719 (0.3%) |
| ALCX-USD | 3,599 | 0 | 4 | 0 | 0 |
| AURORA-USD | 3,599 | 0 | 3 | 0 | 0 |

All 33 failures are false alarms: 15 runs of adjacent failures (13 BTC, 2 SOL), each reconciling exactly once merged.

**Why 2.2% matters.** BTC is the benchmark for every coin (`paired_sample` needs both), and `TargetEvaluator._quality`
needs every grid point from anchor to horizon. So one BTC veto removes every label whose window touches it. If these
proofs became `Sample` coverage, the anchors left clean of trade-ID vetoes would be:

| Anchors with no veto in window | 5m | 15m |
|---|---:|---:|
| BTC, ALCX, AURORA (strict) | 41.4% (273/659) | **3.0%** (16/539) |
| SOL (strict) | 37.2% (245/659) | 3.0% (16/539) |
| All products, merged reconciliation (below) | 100% | 100% |

**It is also selective.** Vetoed BTC buckets had a median of 91 trades against 22 for clean buckets (n=16). Labels would be
lost mainly around active BTC tape, which is when a move radar most needs them. That is missing-not-at-random, not noise.

Limits: this is one hour, descriptive, and counterfactual, because the monitor is not wired into `Sample` today. These
shares count trade-ID vetoes only; quote freshness, warmup and proof latency remove further rows.

**Status update: adopted by the user and implemented on `claude/instant-coverage-reconciliation`** as coverage
contract amendment 1 (limits: 4 proofs, 10,000ms, 100,000 IDs). Correction to the cap remark below: merging the smallest
closing run splits the run of 4 into two pairs, so the largest merge actually needed on this hour is 3 proofs.

**Recommended amendment.** Allow a bounded reconciliation of adjacent failed proofs on one connection:
* The merged span must satisfy the same exact rule: expected IDs in `(low, high]` equal the unique IDs received in it.
* Merge only `missing_live_match_ids` and `receipt_ids_outside_watermarks`, never a heartbeat gap, regression, ID-span or
  conflict failure. A truly lost ID still fails.
* Bucket counts stay attributed by first receipt, so a race does not move any trade.
* Availability becomes the later heartbeat's processing time, so nothing is backdated.
* Predeclare the cap. The longest run observed is 4 proofs (two consecutive races); a cap of 3 would leave that one vetoed.
  Set the cap before the next pilot, from engineering reasoning rather than from any outcome.

The alternative of keeping the strict rule means accepting the table above, and should be chosen knowingly.

## Reproduced defects (fixed; each failed on unmodified `4ed9045`)

**C1 — medium. The real recorder's failure markers were ignored** (`CoverageMonitor._ingest`).
`scripts/coinbase_feed_audit.py` writes `audit_transport_error` and `audit_parse_error`. The monitor handled only
`connection_closed`, `error` and `queue_overflow`, and these markers carry no `product_id`, so they fell through silently.
* Reproduction: `simple()`, then `audit_transport_error` at 2100, then `assess(1000, 2000, asof=2101)`.
* Expected: `transport_observed_ok=False`, and no pair straddling the marker. Actual: `True`, and the pair straddled it.
* Fix: treat `audit_*` as failure markers, the same rule `coinbase_feed_audit.py:130` already uses. Completed proofs are
  not rewritten. **Consequence for the recorder:** a new acknowledgement is required afterwards, so a live connection
  that merely logged a parse error stays unproven until it resubscribes or reconnects. A lost book frame makes this
  essential for the coming `level2_batch` adapter.
* Probes: `test_real_recorder_failure_markers_invalidate_transport`, `test_real_recorder_failure_markers_require_a_new_ack_and_baseline`.

**C2 — low likelihood, but a false pass. `assess` joined proofs by timestamp alone** (`CoverageMonitor.assess`).
Two sessions touching at one millisecond joined into continuous coverage, hiding executions between their watermarks.
* Reproduction: `hb(1000,10)`, `trade(1200,11)`, `hb(1500,11)`, then connection 2: `ack(1500)`, `hb(1500,20)`, `hb(2000,20)`.
* Expected: `[1000,2000)` not proven, since trades 12–20 were never seen. Actual: `receipt_id_check_passed=True`.
* Fix: joined spans must share `connection_id`, and each lower watermark must equal the previous upper. Same-connection
  coverage is unaffected, and each session's own interval still passes.
* Probes: `test_proofs_join_only_when_connection_and_watermark_continue`, `test_contiguous_heartbeats_on_one_connection_still_join`.

**C3 — low. An old-session receipt could satisfy a new session's check** (`CoverageMonitor._ingest`).
The contract says `same_connection`, but IDs were selected by receipt time only, so a trade received on connection 1 at the
new baseline's millisecond counted for connection 2.
* Fix: retain each trade's connection and select only the proof's own. Conservative: a redelivery on the new connection
  is a duplicate, so that interval fails rather than passes.
* Probe: `test_old_session_receipt_cannot_satisfy_new_session_id_check`.

**Real-data effect of the fixes:** none. All 14,396 real proofs are identical apart from processing time. The edits change
`implementation_sha256`; nothing is frozen against it.

## Open items for the publication-timing amendment and next adapter

* **D2 — late negative evidence and labels.** `paired_sample` is pinned to `asof=cutoff`. That is right for inputs. But a
  failure discovered later leaves `Sample.coverage_verified=True`, including on rows used as the forward *outcome* path,
  even though label maturity comes after the discovery (`test_late_negative_evidence_cannot_change_the_decision_time_sample`).
  Decide whether grading may veto on evidence known by maturity. I recommend yes: inputs stay cutoff-only, labels become
  `unavailable`. Today's design cannot do this because the sample discards that evidence.
* **D3 — "known by the cutoff" covers health, not rows.** The monitor measures processing completion. `ReplayStore` stores
  no processing time for quotes or trades, so a row received 1ms before the cutoff but processed after it still counts
  (`test_sample_availability_reflects_health_not_row_processing`). The policy says the adapter does not strengthen trust, but
  the contract's `both_coin_and_benchmark_known_by_the_same_cutoff` reads stronger. The next recorder needs per-row availability.
* **D4 — packet bound versus the book channel (estimate, not measured).** `MAX_PACKETS` is 100,000 and the capture without
  a book already used 63,258 in an hour. One product's 50ms batches can add up to 72,000 an hour, so the one-hour pilot
  could fail closed by exceeding the bound. `quote_channel="level2_batch"` is accepted today, so raise the bound or define
  rotation before the pilot.
* **D5 — connection identity depends on the recorder.** `coinbase_feed_audit.py:55` hard-codes `connection_id: 1`. Today's
  runs are single-connection and write a close marker, so this is safe, but connection-change protection is inert until the
  recorder assigns real IDs.
* **D6 — one connection at a time.** Every change of `connection_id` resets all state. The policy cites Coinbase's advice to
  distribute heavy subscriptions across connections; interleaved connections would never pass. The current one-profile
  plan is fine; a split needs per-connection state.

## Confirmed correct

| Area | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Never backdates | The real CLI run reported `first_proof_available_ts_ms` at today's clock, not the capture's. A same-ms receipt is excluded, and availability uses completion (existing tests pass). |
| ID-set rule | Bounds plus an exact count mean a verified proof is set equality. Duplicates, REST and `last_match` cannot repair it (read and existing probes). Across the real capture, the only failures were the races above. |
| Real envelope shape | The CLI completes on the preserved capture: 63,258 packets, 24,424 unique matches (matching the replay report), maximum heartbeat gap 1,223ms, source hash unchanged. |
| Fail-closed artifact | Bad sequence, clock, amount or missing metadata fails the artifact; truncated and empty inputs are refused (existing tests pass). |
| Quote non-claim | `full_feed_verified` and `quote_coverage_verified` stay False; proofs are not an `add_health` drop-in by shape. |
| `paired_sample` | Grid and as-of checks are enforced by `snapshot`; no later as-of path exists; unavailable rows stay `sample=None`; both legs and the quote-crossing check are required. `input_available_after_cutoff` is defensive and unreachable through `ReplayStore`, since `asof=cutoff` filters later evidence. |

## Tests run (Windows, Python 3.12, project `.venv`)

* The documented targeted list plus `tests/test_instant_coverage.py` and the new probes: **414 passed** (Codex's 405 plus 9 probes).
* The 5 probe cases for C1–C3 failed on unmodified `4ed9045` before the fixes.
* `bash scripts/validate_mw_guardrails.sh`: all guardrails passed.
* Not run: `black` is not installed here, so formatting is unchecked. Frozen historical hashes were not recomputed; no
  historical artifact was touched. Not a full-suite claim. No live feed, REST reconciliation or second capture was exercised.

fixed in `instant_book.py` with 9 changed lines. The publication module has no defect.

## Confirmed defects (each failed on unmodified `3ad47b3`; fixed)

**B1 — medium. The view used a heartbeat before the book had finished processing it** (`BookReplay.view`, `ingest`).
`CoverageMonitor.ingest` stamps its transport record with the monitor's own completion time. `BookReplay` keeps
processing that same envelope and stamps its own consequences later, at book completion. A heartbeat-gap invalidation is
one of them. `view` filtered transport by the monitor's time and book state by the book's time. For an as-of in between,
the gap heartbeat proved a book "fresh" that the same packet invalidates.

* Reproduction: `live()` leaves heartbeat 1500 and snapshot 600. A heartbeat then arrives at 9600, a gap of 8100ms.
  Its clocks are monitor start 9600, monitor done 10000 and book done 10500. Then `view(10000)` is called.
* Expected: unavailable. The latest fully processed heartbeat, 1500, is stale, and the 9600 packet requires a snapshot.
  Actual: `book_state_available=True`, `reason=None`, quote `100.5/101 3/1`, observation age 9400ms.
* Without that heartbeat, the same view is `stale_heartbeat`. An unprocessed packet raised the view to usable, which
  breaks "processing completion measured after book construction".
* The same root cause also acts in the fail-closed direction. A `connection_closed` with book completion at 7000 already
  vetoed `view(5000)` at 4001, as `heartbeat_unavailable`.
* Fix: record each packet's book completion by `receipt_seq` (`self.completed`). A transport record now counts only once
  both the monitor and the book have finished with its packet, so one packet has one knowledge time. Monitor records,
  code and contract are untouched.
* Probes: `test_gap_heartbeat_cannot_freshen_a_book_before_its_invalidation_is_known` and
  `test_one_packet_has_one_knowledge_time_for_transport_and_book`.
* Controls: `test_control_without_the_gap_heartbeat_the_view_is_stale`,
  `test_control_ordinary_heartbeat_is_still_usable_while_the_next_one_is_in_flight` and
  `test_late_failure_is_visible_only_after_processing_and_never_rewrites_earlier`.
* Likelihood: the default clock is milliseconds. The window opens only when book completion falls in a later millisecond
  than monitor completion, and the as-of falls inside that window. Rare on replay, but it is a false pass.

**B2 — low. A size change that only altered the text moved the BBO clock** (`BookReplay._consume`).
`book_change` compares `Decimal` levels, but `bbo_change` compared the string tuple returned by `_best`. `"3"` → `"3.00"`,
or deleting `"100.5"` and re-adding `"100.50"`, changed no price or quantity. It still moved `bbo_change_ts_ms` to 1600
while `book_change_ts_ms` stayed at 600. That is an impossible state, since a BBO change implies a book change, and it
breaks "only an actual level/BBO change moves the corresponding change clock".

* Fix: compare the BBO as `Decimal` amounts. The displayed strings still show the latest spelling.
* Probes: `test_numerically_identical_bbo_does_not_move_change_clocks` (two cases).
* Control: `test_control_real_bbo_size_change_moves_both_clocks`.

Pre-patch run: these 4 probe cases failed and the other 54 passed. Post-patch: all 58 pass, and so do Codex's
54 original book/publication cases, unchanged.

## Verified without defect

1. **Oracles** (hand-calculated mid and `(bid_size−ask_size)/(bid_size+ask_size)`):
   * Absolute replacement: 5 → 2, not 7.
   * Zero deletion, and the next level surfacing after a deletion.
   * Three repeated changes at one price, last change wins: pressure 7/11.
   * Deep-level updates change the book clock but not the BBO clock.
   * A transient cross removed within the same batch is an observation only.
   * Final locked and final crossed books, at the top or deeper, are invalid. An exhausted side is invalid.
   * Deleting an absent level is an observation only.
2. **Sessions:**
   * An ack covering only product A leaves product B unconfirmed.
   * An invalid batch on one product does not affect another.
   * An ack carrying `level2` but no `level2_batch` does not confirm the book.
   * REST/`rest_recovery` snapshots carrying another `connection_id`, and ws tickers, change nothing.
   * `queue_overflow`, `error` and `audit_*` invalidate every product. Each then needs a fresh ack and then a snapshot.
   * The heartbeat gap is strictly greater than 5000ms: 6500 is allowed and 6501 invalidates.
   * A same-millisecond heartbeat fails closed until the next beat.
3. **Clocks:**
   * A receipt at 4999 is included at cutoff 5000. A receipt at 5000 is excluded, even at a later as-of.
   * Heartbeat freshness is exactly 5000ms. The observation TTL is exactly 10000ms, and heartbeats never renew it.
   * A late failure appears only at its completion and does not rewrite earlier as-of views.
   * A queued packet leaves the older processed quote visible, as documented; that is not a completeness certificate.
   * A regressing or non-integer book clock fails the artifact, and queries are then refused.
   * Queries require a grid cutoff and an integer as-of no earlier than the cutoff.
   * Results are frozen, and their hashes match the LF-normalised sources.
4. **Publication:**
   * Hand-calculated ages are 150/40/80ms. Equal neighbouring clocks are allowed.
   * Age bounds are exact: 149, 150 and 0. A bound never enables fitting.
   * Inputs at the cutoff with a late publication still flag the amendment.
   * Non-string IDs, float or negative clocks, and every order violation are rejected.
   * Arguments are keyword-only, results are frozen, and the source hash is correct.
   * The cutoff and publication anchors stay distinct. No lag policy is frozen.
5. **Offline:** a clean `-I` interpreter imports both modules without `socket`, `ssl`, `http`, `urllib.request`,
   `requests`, `websocket(s)`, `aiohttp` or backend modules. The level and TTL constants are unchanged.

## Design proposals (not implemented, not defects)

* **P1 — retrospective gap.** A heartbeat gap that ends after a cutoff is not applied backwards to that cutoff, even at
  a later as-of. That is consistent with the contract. A later grading policy should decide whether later-known gap
  evidence vetoes an earlier sample.
* **P2 — first beat after a snapshot.** The gap timer starts at the first heartbeat, not at the ack or snapshot. A long
  silence between snapshot and first heartbeat is caught only by `heartbeat_unavailable` and the 10s TTL. Consider
  starting the gap clock at the ack.
* **P3 — in-flight count.** `view` could report how many receipts before the cutoff were still unprocessed at the as-of.
  That would let queue diagnostics avoid treating an older quote as complete.
* **P4 — contract wording.** The contract's `recovery` text lists queue loss under "new snapshot". The implementation and
  the Markdown both also require a fresh ack, which is stricter. Clarify the text; the behaviour was not changed.
* **P5 — absent-level deletes.** Deleting an absent level is tolerated. It may signal a desynchronised book, so consider
  counting it as a diagnostic.

## Limitations and unverified concerns

* **Connection identity.** The recorder writes a constant `connection_id=1`. Real reconnect identity and multi-socket
  routing are not implemented upstream, and nothing here claims them.
* **Real-data frequency of B1.** It was not measured, because no real `level2_batch` capture exists.
* **Out of scope.** `Sample` integration, the continuous F3 integral and throughput are documented limitations, not
  defects.

## Checks

* **Runtime:** cloud Linux, Python 3.12.3. Pinned 3.12.10 was unavailable, so this is the closest 3.12. pytest 8.3.5,
  Black 24.8.0.
* **Targeted suite: 579 passed.** That is Codex's 521-test command from the association acceptance plus these 58 probes.
* Black 24.8.0 is clean on all four book/publication files. Repository guardrails pass.
* All 11 historical hashes in `live_movers_contract_20261027/SHA256SUMS.json` match.
* **Protected files:** `git diff 3ad47b3` touches only `instant_book.py`. This covers the targets and V1/V2,
  diagnostics, monitor, samples, association, recorder, verifier, `docs/research/results`, backend and frontend, and all
  the contracts. V1 stays the default, and the held `d711832` stays unmerged.
* Not run: the full test folder, native Windows/Mac, and anything with real data.

# Reconciliation review: continuity boundary blocker

Resolution, November 4: `d26c57d` fixes the reproduced blocker and `1af0a64`
adds a second probe set. Codex accepted them through `055d590`; 1,064 targeted
tests pass. Read [the acceptance](INSTANT_RECONCILIATION_ACCEPTANCE_20261104.md).
The original reproduction below remains a record of unmodified `bfab9c8`.

November 4, 2026. Proposed commit `bfab9c86394a9117951b8f4fb970da88a34a7e32`
was inspected read-only in local Claude's worktree. It has **not been merged**
into `codex/instant-radar-20261028`. Codex reproduced one blocking violation;
this is not a complete acceptance of the remaining implementation or its
reported 645 checks. The root branch's existing 521-check result is unchanged.

## Confirmed false pass

All events below use the same connection ID. Receipt sequence and monotonic
time are nondecreasing, with strictly increasing local sequence numbers.

| Receipt ms | Event |
|---:|---|
| 900 | Acknowledge ticker, matches, heartbeat for TEST-USD |
| 1000 | Heartbeat, last trade ID 10 |
| 2000 | Heartbeat, last trade ID 11: first interval fails, missing ID 11 |
| 2000 | `audit_parse_error`: continuity must reset |
| 2000 | New complete subscription acknowledgement |
| 2000 | New heartbeat baseline, last trade ID 11 |
| 2500 | Live match ID 11, price 100, size 1, maker side sell |
| 3000 | Heartbeat, last trade ID 11: second interval has an out-of-watermark ID |

Actual result on `bfab9c8`: a merged proof covers `[1000,3000)` and
`assess('TEST-USD',1000,3000,asof=3001)['receipt_id_check_passed']` is **True**.
Expected: the audit failure separates the two continuity epochs, so those
proofs must never merge, even with equal receipt milliseconds, the same
connection ID and touching watermarks. Individually valid intervals on either
side may still be used after recovery. Exact union ID equality alone does
not remove an explicit continuity failure.

The cause is `_reconcile` checking connection ID, touching receipt intervals
and watermarks without retaining an epoch or detecting the intervening failure
in receipt-sequence order. The same condition must be checked when `assess`
joins otherwise valid intervals. Actual recorder connection IDs remain a
separate upstream requirement; resubscription on one live connection can
legitimately retain its ID.

## Required follow-up on Claude's branch

Keep the bounded caps, original failed proofs, receipt-attributed trades and
actual reconciliation availability. Add continuity epochs or explicit lifecycle
barriers identified by receipt sequence. No merge or assessment chain may span
an audit/queue/transport failure or lost required subscription, even when all
events share a millisecond and recovery reuses a connection ID.

First add this counterexample against unmodified `bfab9c8`; also test transport
errors, queue loss and lost/readded subscription membership at a shared
timestamp. Test valid recovery on each side, healthy repeated acknowledgements,
ordinary races without a barrier, and known-at immutability. Run the relevant
new tests, original coverage/book tests, formatting and guardrails. Preserve
other agents' work and push the correction on Claude's own branch for Codex
verification. Do not change live services, targets, outcome studies or limits
to hide the failure. No new capture or model fit is required.

The next cloud assignment remains the independent book/publication review;
it should not duplicate this local reconciliation correction.

# Claude independent review of the opt-in directional exposure report — 2026-11-04

Assignment: [CLAUDE_DIRECTIONAL_EXPOSURE_REVIEW_20261104.md](../../ai/CLAUDE_DIRECTIONAL_EXPOSURE_REVIEW_20261104.md).
Cloud session, own branch `claude/instant-directional-exposure-review-20261104`.
**Pending Codex review; not merged.** `codex/instant-radar-20261028` remains authoritative, and its
checkpoint files (`RESUME_HERE.md`, `ACTIVE_WORK.json`, `HANDOFF.md`) are untouched on this branch.

- **Inspected tip:** `f5617066397d26191d342b1956590aee65bc0f48` (docs-only on top of implementation `9da4987`).
- **Reviewed, byte-identical to Codex's `instant_directional_exposure_20261104/CHECKS.json`:**
  `instant_directional_exposure.py` `638db051…`, its contract `6bc66818…`, `tests/test_instant_directional_exposure.py` `2a528753…`.
- **Read-only dependencies:** `instant_association.py` `0e79b8f1…` and its contract `4a66c231…`, both unchanged since the accepted
  `8631231`.
- **Probes:** `tests/test_instant_directional_exposure_review.py`, 51 cases.
- **Checks record:** [CHECKS.json](../results/claude_directional_exposure_review_20261104/CHECKS.json).

**Verdict: no defect found.** Every property in the assignment holds under independent hand calculation, and seven deliberate
mutants of the module each fail at least one probe, so the probes are sensitive to the behaviours they claim to pin. No fix was
needed, so there is no patch and no pre-patch failure to report; the pre-patch evidence is that all 51 probes pass on the
unmodified pushed implementation. Four small robustness and policy suggestions follow; none is a contract violation.

## Verified by hand

Oracle universe: products A, B, C at four 5s cutoffs T0..T3, 24 eligibility records, with C@T0 outside the universe on both
boards and direction-specific flags elsewhere. Expected values were written before running the code.

1. **Product-hours and time unions.** Up board eligible {A@T0, A@T1, B@T1, C@T3}: 4 slots = 20 s of product-time,
   cutoff union {T0, T1, T3} = 15 s. Down board eligible {B@T0, B@T2, C@T2}: 15 s and union {T0, T2} = 10 s. Either-board union
   {T0, T1, T2, T3} = 20 s, strictly less than the 25 s sum. Universe sizes [2, 3, 3, 3]. Exclusions by first failed flag:
   up {1, 3, 1, 3}, down {1, 1, 3, 4}. All match.
2. **Ranker complete rows.** Three rankers with different coverage per board. Up board: R1∩R2∩R3 = {A@T1}; at 300 s it is
   gradable so the identical-row table is `[[A, T1]]`; at 900 s A@T1 is `unavailable:x`, so the table is empty. Down board: R3
   scored nothing there, so no row is scored by every ranker, and the natural per-ranker coverage still reports R1 1/1, R2 3/2,
   R3 0/0. Without rankers the table is every gradable row, as documented; with a ranker that scored nothing it is empty.
3. **Own-direction gradability.** A ready up failure stays gradable whether the down label is missing, pending, unavailable,
   or the down board is flagged ineligible. Both boards grade independently with opposite outcomes. The legacy joint reporter
   still requires both labels on the same inputs.
4. **Exposure independent of future labels.** The exposure keys of every board, the universe sizes and the either-board union
   are identical with no labels and with all 48 label rows ready.
5. **Unavailable never becomes a negative.** The directional output contains no success, failure, negative or
   `verified_forward` field anywhere; an unavailable own-direction label is counted only under its reason.
6. **Horizon maturity boundaries.** At as-of = anchor + 299,999 both horizons are immature; at + 300,000 only 300 s is mature;
   at + 899,999 still only 300 s; at + 900,000 both. Decision eligibility is unaffected throughout.
7. **Clock types and boundaries.** Report as-of and `available_ts_ms` reject bool, float, negative, string and None.
   `available_ts_ms == ts_ms` and `ts_ms == asof` are accepted; `available_ts_ms = ts_ms + 1` and off-grid or bool cutoffs are
   rejected.
8. **Pairing, universe, duplicates, flags.** Pairing is per product *and* cutoff. Missing or integer flags are rejected for every
   flag. Unknown directions are rejected. A duplicate ranker slot given once as a tuple and once as a list is still a duplicate.
   Mixed label provenance is rejected even when the odd row belongs to a slot that is not on any board.
9. **Empty and immutable.** Empty labels, eligibility and rankers at as-of 0 give a zero report with null fractions. Labels,
   eligibility and ranker inputs are not mutated.
10. **Legacy and provenance.** The report embeds `instant_association.provenance()` unchanged, hashes its own LF-normalised
    source and contract correctly, and the joint reporter's output shape is untouched.

Mutants killed by the probes: ready failure made ungradable (6 probes fail), maturity boundary off by one (2), ranker union
instead of intersection (1), availability checked against as-of instead of cutoff (1), either-board sum instead of union (1),
pairing check skipped (1), opposite-direction label required (4).

## The limitation, reviewed explicitly

The contract and the module both say eligibility is `caller_assertions_not_authenticated_feed_evidence`, and that is accurate.
Two probes make the limitation executable rather than a sentence:

- The reporter accepts a record claiming `available_ts_ms = 0` for a cutoff an hour later. It cannot know that is false.
- Submitting only A and B at T0 yields a universe of size 2. A product omitted from both boards is invisible; the pairing check
  only catches a product that is present on one board and not the other.

So this reporter does not produce authenticated eligibility and does not prove the intended universe is complete. Any claim to
that effect should be rejected until the separately versioned evidence producer exists, with per-row processing completion
and an intended-inventory record. I did not implement a feed bridge or any producer; that is future work by design.

## Suggestions (not defects)

- **S1, robustness.** A malformed ranker slot shape (two items, four items, a dict, a bare string) raises `TypeError` from
  `_key(*record)` rather than the module's usual `ValueError`. It still rejects; a shape check before unpacking would make the
  failure message consistent. A non-mapping `ranker_slots` similarly raises `AttributeError`.
- **S2, association module, out of scope here.** `_labels` accepts pending or unavailable labels anchored after the report
  as-of; the directional reporter simply ignores them because no eligibility can exist there. Rejecting `anchor > asof` for all
  statuses would make the two modules' causality checks symmetric. No behaviour of the reviewed module depends on it.
- **S3, study policy.** A ranker that scored no rows on one board collapses that board's identical-row set to empty. That is
  the stated definition, but a caller comparing one-direction rankers may not expect it. Reporting the number of rankers with
  any eligible score on the board would make such tables self-explanatory.
- **S4, documentation.** `universe_size_by_cutoff` is derived from up-board membership. That is sound only because the pairing
  check enforces equal membership; a one-line note in the contract would prevent a future reader from loosening one without
  the other.

## Checks

- **Runtime:** cloud Linux, Python 3.12.3 (pinned 3.12.10 unavailable), pytest 8.3.5, Black 24.8.0.
- **Directional and association tests:** 101 passed (48 + 53). **Review probes:** 51 passed.
- **Targeted set, Codex's recorded command:** 1,163 passed (Codex's 1,112 plus these 51).
- Black clean on the directional module, association module and both directional test files. Guardrails pass.
- All 11 historical hashes in `live_movers_contract_20261027/SHA256SUMS.json` match.
- **Protected sources:** `git diff 3c859ef HEAD` outside the three new directional files and their checks record touches only
  documentation. Targets, V1/V2, diagnostics, monitor, samples, book, publication, association, recorder, results, backend,
  frontend and every existing contract are byte-identical.
- Not run: the full test folder, native Windows or Mac, and anything with real data. No capture, outcome analysis, fitting,
  historical rerun, threshold change, live scoring or production change.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 19 | docs/research/reviews/CLAUDE_INPUT_EVIDENCE_REVIEW_20261104.md | sha256=83e2ec891ba875c420fd969af0f81248f97aa71809d1aac813f7fafd19a379f1 =====
# Claude independent review of input-evidence stage 1 — 2026-11-04

Assignment: [CLAUDE_INPUT_EVIDENCE_REVIEW_20261104.md](../../ai/CLAUDE_INPUT_EVIDENCE_REVIEW_20261104.md).
Cloud session, own branch `claude/instant-input-evidence-review-20261104`. **Pending Codex review; not merged.**
`codex/instant-radar-20261028` remains authoritative; its checkpoint files are untouched on this branch.

- **Inspected tip:** `fee55a7dd2bbb32c661c531d6fe9a8432db9400d`, documentation-only on top of implementation `8607a36`.
- **Reviewed, byte-identical to Codex's `instant_input_evidence_20261104/CHECKS.json`:** `instant_input_evidence.py` `f507998c…`,
  its contract `beb5492f…`, `tests/test_instant_input_evidence.py` `240ef221…`.
- **Read-only dependencies, unchanged since their accepted reviews:** `instant_book.py` `c06834d7…`, `instant_coverage.py` `0682e134…`,
  `instant_directional_exposure.py` `638db051…`.
- **Probes:** `tests/test_instant_input_evidence_review.py`, 46 cases, with an independent tape helper that sets watermarks,
  connections and the five-step per-packet processing clock explicitly.
- **Checks record:** [CHECKS.json](../results/claude_input_evidence_review_20261104/CHECKS.json).

**Verdict: no defect found.** All 46 probes pass on the unmodified pushed implementation, so there is no patch and the pre-patch
evidence is that clean run. Ten deliberate mutants were run against the probes: eight are killed, and the two survivors are
equivalent mutants explained by an ordering invariant the probes pin (below). The scope statements in the contract and the
Markdown are accurate and are now executable facts rather than prose.

## Verified

1. **Inventory.** Rows are exactly products × every 5 s cutoff in `[first, stop)` × both directions; the inventory hash is
   independent of product order. A planned product with no packets keeps both rows with explicit `coin:quote:` and
   `coin:trades:` reasons, and its benchmark component is still evaluated. The row cap is inclusive at 10,000; 10,004 is
   rejected, as are a first cutoff below 30 s, off-grid or inverted ranges, empty or non-string products and 33 products.
2. **Declaration time versus plan hash.** Two recorders loading the same plan share one `inventory_sha256`; whether the plan
   "precedes" the first cutoff depends only on the measured load time. Loading at 29,999 precedes a 30,000 cutoff; at 30,000 it
   does not, and every row then carries `inventory_recorded_after_first_cutoff`.
3. **Payload completion is the gate, not receipt, book or monitor completion.**
   * Late merged proof: a heartbeat overtakes match 11, the match arrives, and the closing heartbeat at 30,000 is processed with
     monitor completion 30,002 but producer completion 30,500. The frozen 30,000 row records the then-known failed proof
     (`missing_live_match_ids`, count unknown). At as-of 30,100 the monitor already reports the merged proof
     (`proof_available_ts_ms = 30002`), yet the producer says `trade_row_processing_incomplete` and keeps the count null. At
     30,500 it reports one verified trade and 200 USD. The frozen row is unchanged afterwards.
   * Quote: a heartbeat at 29,999 whose book and monitor processing finish inside the cutoff but whose producer processing
     finishes at 30,001 makes the book view available while the component reports `quote_row_processing_incomplete` with no
     bid; at 30,001 the quote is available with the snapshot's own book completion time (25,011), not the heartbeat's.
   * A packet processed in the same millisecond it was received is available at receipt + 1 ms, and the envelope hash is the
     canonical JSON hash.
4. **Deduplication and sides.** The first receipt wins across connections even when the duplicate changes spelling
   (`100.00`/`2.0`); a conflicting duplicate vetoes the window. Maker sides are inverted; an unknown side keeps its amount
   separate, still counts in the verified total, and blocks `flow_available`.
5. **Epochs at a shared millisecond.** A trade received at 0 on connection 1, followed at 0 by a connection change (or a
   `queue_overflow` with resubscription), is kept in its original epoch, counted as known, and never certified by the new
   epoch's complete proof chain: `trade_row_continuity_mismatch`, count null.
6. **Verified zeros versus unknown.** With heartbeats but no snapshot, trades are a proven zero at 30,001 while the quote is
   unavailable; at the cutoff itself the zero is still unknown because the closing beat is known at 30,001. A stale quote keeps
   its original observation (age 29,990 ms) while the trade component independently reports a proven zero. A missing benchmark
   marks every row, including BTC's own, and the coin's fresh book is not inherited.
7. **Frozen cohorts.** `SlotEvidence` is frozen; decoded copies are independent; later trades, an audit failure and sealing
   leave the cohort object and its decoded rows identical. A checkpoint taken late, after more packets, yields the same
   components as one taken at the cutoff. Assessment completion is measured separately from knowledge as-of.
8. **Failures.** Float, boolean, negative or string clocks, a tape not starting at sequence 1, a sequence gap and the monitor's
   packet cap each fail the artifact closed; checkpoint, seal and report then refuse. Component queries reject float or
   boolean cutoffs, unplanned cutoffs and as-of before the cutoff.
9. **CLI.** Output nested inside the input directory is refused before anything is created. A valid run records the plan and
   tape hashes, 4 planned rows all `unavailable`, and a packet ledger of the right length. A tape with a sequence gap leaves
   `FAILURE.json` naming the sequence failure and no `REPORT.json`.

Mutants killed: quote producer gate dropped, proof heartbeat producer gate dropped, epoch ignored in continuity, predeclaration
boundary `<=`, taker side not inverted, cohort not cached, first-sequence check removed, deduplication by `seen` replaced.

## Scope, reviewed as executable facts

* **No positive eligibility path.** A fully healthy synthetic tape (fresh snapshots, continuous heartbeats, proven windows) still
  yields every row `unavailable`, `study_eligible = False`, with the four not-implemented reasons present, and every report flag
  (`full_feed_verified`, `positive_eligibility_supported`, `directional_report_adapter_supported`,
  `durable_publication_verified`, `study_frozen`, `feed_gate_pass`, `fitting_allowed`, `live_integration`) false.
* **An empty tape accounts for every planned row but proves nothing.** Zero packets, eight rows, every component unavailable.
* **Completeness is relative to the plan.** A product present on the wire but absent from the plan produces no row and no
  warning; `expected_rows == recorded_rows` holds by construction. That equality is an accounting assertion, not a check that
  can fail. The intended exchange universe still needs an external, predeclared inventory.
* **Nothing is strengthened elsewhere.** The module imports only the book and monitor; a clean `-I` interpreter loads it without
  the directional reporter, association, targets, diagnostics, replay store, sockets or backend code. Directional and target
  hashes are unchanged.
* Baseline and feature evidence, continuous quote health, durable publication and full-feed certification are not produced
  here; the trade window is a 30 s receipt-ID check and the quote is a last processed state with its original age.

## Observations and suggestions (not defects)

- **O1, ordering invariant.** Under sequential ingest with a monotone clock, a trade row's producer completion is always at or
  before the closing heartbeat's completion, so the `len(known) == len(rows)` branch of `proof_rows_known` cannot fire on its own
  (mutants M4 and M9 survive for this reason). The reachable gate is the closing heartbeat's producer completion, which is
  tested. The branch is harmless and defensive; keep it, but the docs should not describe it as a separate observable case.
- **O2, reason precedence.** When the producer has not finished a quote packet, `quote.reason` is `quote_row_processing_incomplete`
  even if the book's own reason (for example `stale_book_observation`) would also apply. Fail-closed; reporting both would help
  diagnostics.
- **O3, failed runs and cached cohorts.** After a failure, `checkpoint` refuses even already-frozen cohorts. Consistent with "a
  failed or partial output is not an eligibility artifact"; worth a sentence in the contract so it is not read as data loss.
- **O4, Decimal rendering.** Amounts are rendered with `str(Decimal)`, so exotic exchange spellings such as `1E+2` would appear
  in exponent form. Cosmetic.
- **O5, prospective use.** With the real clock, a replayed historical plan can never precede its first cutoff, so every replay row
  carries `inventory_recorded_after_first_cutoff`. That is correct and means a pilot must load its plan before the first cutoff;
  that procedure belongs to the future evidence producer and pilot design, not to this module.

## Checks

- **Runtime:** cloud Linux, Python 3.12.3 (pinned 3.12.10 unavailable), pytest 8.3.5, Black 24.8.0.
- **Existing input-evidence tests:** 32 passed. **Review probes:** 46 passed on unmodified code.
- **Targeted set, Codex's recorded command:** 1,241 passed (Codex's 1,195 plus these 46).
- Black clean on the module, both test files, book and monitor. Guardrails pass.
- All 11 historical hashes in `live_movers_contract_20261027/SHA256SUMS.json` match.
- **Protected sources:** `git diff fd1079d HEAD` over scripts, tests, backend, frontend, results and every contract, excluding the
  three new input-evidence files and their checks record, is empty.
- Not run: the full test folder, native Windows or Mac, any real tape. No capture, outcome study, fit, historical rerun,
  threshold change, pilot, live scoring or production change.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 20 | docs/research/INSTANT_LIVE_TIMING_PILOT_PROTOCOL_20261109.md | sha256=258bed490efcdfbbe768b9960d8d816b2eac252ddbac0d27c11c871e61debd0f =====
# Bounded live timing and eligibility pilot

The [saved-journal comparison](INSTANT_DELAYED_ELIGIBILITY_REPLAY_20261109.md)
found the same complete input slots at +1.5s and +5s. This pilot measures
when a separate local process actually sees those decisions under load.
It also measures how often multiple non-benchmark products are ready at
the same cutoff.

Use the Coinbase Exchange public socket and the existing
[level2_batch, matches, heartbeat and ticker subscriptions](https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/exchange/websocket-feed/channels).
Keep BTC-USD as the benchmark and SOL-USD as a candidate. Retain ALCX-USD
and AURORA-USD in coverage accounting. Add LINK-USD and AVAX-USD as fixed
engineering candidates. Coinbase listed both as online when the roster
was chosen. Readiness and useful sample size are open measurements.

Use one attempt in each of three broad UTC activity periods. The labels
are local shorthand for UTC windows.

| Window | UTC hours | Start order within each pair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `asia` | 00:00–06:00 | +1.5s, +5s |
| `europe` | 07:00–13:00 | +5s, +1.5s |
| `us` | 14:00–20:00 | +1.5s, +5s |

Each attempt uses the `eligibility-timing-256` profile, one connection, a
67-minute duration after full subscription acknowledgment, 256 queued
frames and up to 16 frames per journal batch. The full timing profile stops at 499,990 packets or 480 MiB of logical
records. The journal enforces hard limits of 500,000 packets and 512 MiB.
The original research profiles and the startup smoke keep their existing
limits. Retain the captured prefix and
stop reason if an attempt ends early. Keep enough time inside the window
for subscription and the full capture. Record the implementation commit
and plan before opening the socket. On Windows, take five read-only
clock samples against time.windows.com before and after each attempt.

Begin diagnostics on the first 5s grid cutoff at least 62 minutes after
the original acknowledgment receipt. Run 20 cutoffs 10 seconds apart
with ten assigned to each wait. Each scheduled cutoff has two rows per
product. Use a fixed evidence as-of for that schedule and log actual
preparation, commit and consumer clocks. The independent consumer polls
the outbox every 25ms. Compare the +1.5s schedule with an illustrative
+2.5s consumer age bound, and the +5s reference with +6s. Alternating
cutoffs avoids two decision batches publishing at the same cutoff. The
two schedules see nearby but different market conditions.

Report all planned, committed, observed, observed within bound, and
complete-input rows by product and schedule. Retain missing cutoffs in
the denominators. Report which non-benchmark rows were both complete
and delivered within bound. Report clock integrity, queue pressure,
paired ticker/match sides and any early stop. A capped attempt before
warmup is inconclusive for delivery. A healthy attempt with repeated
late reads or insufficient simultaneous readiness identifies work for
the next pilot. Three bounded sessions can screen for large problems.
Feed certification, the study rules and fitting remain gated.

Run `scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_real_indexed_pilot.py` with
`--profile eligibility-timing-256 --window us --out-dir <new attempt directory>`
or substitute the appropriate UTC window. The separate
`--startup-smoke` mode uses the same six products and a 60-second
capture to check subscription and journal operation before warmup. The
[initial smoke](results/instant_live_timing_20261109/STARTUP_01.json)
confirmed all six subscriptions and committed 5,743 records. Its rate would exhaust
the earlier budget before warmup if sustained, so the full timing profile
uses the higher explicit bounds above. A one-minute rate does not establish
capacity or liquidity across the whole attempt.

Use the implementation pinned with each earlier archive for replay. The
journal verifies monitor, book and input code digests, including capacity
changes in this pilot.

After the final report is present, run
`scripts/scoring_experiment/verify_instant_timing_attempt.py <attempt directory>`
with `--plan-sha256` and the digest from the active-work record.
It verifies the journal and frozen decision prefixes against stored data,
matches observed timestamps to the outbox receipts, and recalculates readiness
and delivery by schedule. It includes every planned slot, plus the number
of cutoffs with two or more ready and on-time non-benchmark products in each
direction. The verifier matched the 166,035-packet archive with 80 published
decisions and the 5,743-packet startup check.
