# Reader queue overflow: offline diagnosis and next PC check

The November 10 Windows Asia attempt ended in a transport error before
warmup. Review the
[verified result](results/instant_reader_overflow_20261110/ASIA_01_VERIFIED.json)
before planning another capture.

The verified remote `origin/codex/instant-radar-20261028` tip was
`fca475e014bab94549fdda7816390a4169b58f7d`, identical to the supplied checkpoint.
There was no newer work on that branch to review. Implementation is
`cfa4aff44aa017de704b7fa8c641dcf7389ef159`, on isolated branch
`codex/instant-radar-overflow-diagnosis-20261110`.

This work satisfies MW_SPEC 0.1 (measured evidence and explicit missingness)
and 0.2 (targeted changes): only research reader/capture diagnostics, offline
tests and checkpoint documentation change. Queue sizes, batch sizes, capture
bounds, causal clocks, journal schema, scoring and production are unchanged.
No socket capture, historical discovery, locked validation or fitting ran.

## What the US evidence establishes

[US_01_VERIFIED.json](results/instant_live_timing_20261109/US_01_VERIFIED.json)
reports `queue_overflow`, a finished segment, SQLite quick check `ok`, 302,679
verified packet records, 302,677 verified batch-ingress clocks, zero pending
ingress frames and zero publications or local delivery observations. The
portable checkpoint records 2,943.474 seconds (49m 03.474s) from full ACK to the
last committed reader receipt. That is before the 62-minute warmup.
All 20 planned timing cohorts were unreached. Zero observations is not a
latency measurement, readiness measurement or evidence of successful delivery.

The full Windows capture/report/stdout was not available in this cloud checkout.
The committed verifier summary contains neither contemporaneous arrival counts
nor writer-phase state. No new claim about that capture's initiating cause can
be recovered from this summary. The earlier successful four-product session
and synthetic capacity trials do not establish six-product US capacity.

## What the old logs can distinguish

| Evidence before this change | Supported conclusion | Remaining ambiguity |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Stop reason and error text | Queue frame cap versus an oversized frame | No burst/stall attribution |
| Received/delivered/high-water counts | Local returns exceeded delivered frames and the queue saturated | Failed enqueue and undelivered backlog are not committed packets |
| Aggregate reader-to-writer latency | Committed frames waited locally | Lost tail is unmeasured; no failure-time ordering |
| Aggregate callback-through-commit timing | A completed journal callback was slow | Processing, SQLite, CPU scheduling and storage delay share the metric |
| Progress/after-commit hooks | Possibly long gaps between progress lines | Hook duration is absent from callback latency; no live phase at overflow |

The test pair reproduces the same old counts (257 received, zero delivered,
256 high-water) with a 257-frame rapid return burst and with paced returns
while the writer is held. The journal integration cases also show that a
blocked post-commit hook can fill the queue while the old callback duration
stays zero under the controlled clock. A journal persistence stall appears in
that old duration once the callback finally completes. Thus the logs contain
some timing hints, but do not uniquely identify the failure-time mechanism.

## Additive instrumentation

`QueuedSocket` retains at most 257 monotonic return timestamps. There is no
per-frame disk log or payload copy. On failed enqueue it freezes one
`instant-reader-overflow-v1` snapshot with:

- Distinct `queue_full` / `frame_bytes_exceeded` reasons, wall receipt label,
  queue capacity/depth, received/delivered counts and oldest queued-frame age.
- Counts of socket returns in the trailing 100ms and 1s, retained sample span
  and `lower_bound` flags when the bounded ring cannot prove an exact count.
  The rejected frame is included in the return count, never in the queue.
- Active owner phase (`journal_callback`, `after_commit`, or
  `between_callbacks`), phase elapsed time, batch frame count and last
  completed owner activity. `between_callbacks` includes uninstrumented
  setup, lifecycle, progress and loop work; it is not proof of an idle CPU.

The reader immediately emits one `READER_OVERFLOW {json}` line to stderr,
before the owner resumes. A completed pilot also stores the same frozen
snapshot at `REPORT.json -> reader[i].overflow`. The healthy instrumented
control has `overflow: null`. The report returns copies, so later shutdown,
writer recovery or caller mutation cannot alter failure-time evidence.
Stderr emission is best effort, not a durable journal receipt; retain it
separately if the owner never completes a final report.

Counts and queue depth are local samples around failed enqueue, not an atomic
transaction with the consumer's queue removal. Arrival windows measure local
`recv()` returns, not exchange sends or physical network ingress. Large counts
can mean exchange traffic, network/OS/socket buffering or local scheduling.
A long `journal_callback` identifies the occupied phase, not disk versus CPU
versus descheduling. A burst and a writer stall can occur together; no automatic
single-cause classifier or capacity recommendation is justified.

## Reproduce offline

From the repository root with the research Python environment:

```powershell
python -m pytest -q tests/test_instant_reader_overflow.py tests/test_instant_real_indexed_pilot.py tests/test_instant_source_pilot.py tests/test_instant_stream_journal.py tests/test_instant_timing_attempt_verifier.py
python -m pytest -q tests/test_instant*.py
bash scripts/validate_mw_guardrails.sh
```

Use Bash for shell glob expansion in the second command, or pass the explicit
file list from your runner on Windows. The unattended saved-attempt runner now
includes `test_instant_reader_overflow.py` as well.

The new 11 cases use Events and a controlled monotonic arrival clock, with
bounded real-time waits only to fail deadlocks. No test depends on thread speed
or an actual sleep. They cover 4- and 256-frame queues, burst and paced input,
a blocked real indexed journal persistence operation, a blocked post-commit
hook, immediate stderr emission before recovery, frozen report copies,
FIFO/no-loss control, oversized-frame distinction, SQLite integrity and the
explicit queue-loss barrier. The writer stall is an injected persistence gate,
not a measurement of actual Windows disk behavior.

In the controlled 256-frame case, 257 returns span 25.6ms for a burst versus
5,120ms for paced arrivals. The latter shows the writer phase occupied for
5,140ms when the failed enqueue occurs. These are declared synthetic durations,
not capacity or real-world latency results. All 1,243 Instant Radar tests pass
on Linux Python 3.12.14 / pytest 8.3.5. Black 24.8.0, repository guardrails and
`git diff --check` pass. See [checks](results/instant_reader_overflow_20261110/CHECKS.json).

This is a future, manually started engineering check, not authorization for an

```powershell
$repo = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE 'Projects\Moonwalking-InstantRadar'
$work = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE 'Projects\Moonwalking-InstantRadarOverflow-20261110'

$pilotExit = $LASTEXITCODE

$clockExit = $LASTEXITCODE
if ($pilotExit -ne 0 -or $clockExit -ne 0) { throw 'Preserve logs; pilot or post-clock check failed. Do not retry automatically.' }
$planSha = (Get-FileHash (Join-Path $attempt 'PLAN.json') -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant()
```

===== BEGIN SOURCE 22 | docs/research/INSTANT_READER_DISCONNECT_REVIEW_20261111.md | sha256=4481dd460ddda86433c23504d3e081658381bdcd7a7167d19a928368a550a550 =====
# Asia disconnect review: saved evidence and offline probes

The remote overflow-diagnosis branch was verified at
`598fba0bc7d6c2fc754f6bb22cbeedce2f79b394`, matching the Windows checkpoint.
Verification checkpoint is `975234ed44c6708a28ccce1df11ed671258cd46c`.
That newer commit was reviewed before starting isolated branch
`codex/instant-radar-disconnect-diagnosis-20261111`. It preserves the Asia
attempt and changes documentation, not reader implementation.

This review follows MW_SPEC 0.1 (measured evidence, explicit missingness) and
0.2 (targeted changes). Ten offline regression probes and the saved-attempt
test runner change; no reader or capture runtime fix is warranted by the
reproductions below. No live capture, capacity change, reconnect change,
production change, historical validation or fitting ran.

## Evidence and limits

[ASIA_01_VERIFIED.json](results/instant_reader_overflow_20261110/ASIA_01_VERIFIED.json)
is the committed evidence. The original full capture and logs remain on Windows;
this cloud review did not independently reopen that capture.

- The verifier reports 167,407 journal records, 167,405 verified batch-ingress
  clocks, SQLite quick check `ok`, a finished segment and no pending ingress.
- The PC reader returned and delivered 167,405 frames; sampled high water was
  231 of 256, `overflow` was null, and stderr was empty. A disconnect is not
  evidence of queue saturation, and delivered frames are not a proof that no
  exchange messages were missed before reaching the local socket.
- Full ACK was at `1794356673393`; the last reader receipt was
  `1794359028291` (2026-11-11 01:03:48.291 UTC / November 10 17:03:48.291 Pacific).
  Their difference is 39m 14.898s, before the 62-minute warmup. This timestamps
  the last successful frame, not the exact instant of socket failure.
- `stop=transport_error`, with text `Connection to remote host was lost.`
  All 20 cohorts were unreached. Zero publications/observations means timing
  and readiness remain unmeasured, not zero latency or a successful pilot.
- The immediate post-run `time.windows.com` clock probe reported DNS failure
  `0x80072AF9`. A separate retry returned five samples. Preserve both results:
  the retry does not turn the original check into a pass. Their relation to
  the earlier already-established Coinbase connection is unknown.

The verifier proves the recorded prefix and denominators under its checks. It
does not diagnose the network, certify clock synchronization through a DNS
failure, or identify who terminated a socket.

## What the reader does on disconnect

The new `tests/test_instant_reader_disconnect.py` probes use a finished-reader
barrier and simulated receipt clocks. They test single-frame and 16-frame
journal paths with the actual `QueuedSocket` and indexed SQLite journal:

| Input | Verified behavior |
| --- | --- |
| WebSocket connection-lost exception | Retains exception, drains every queued frame in FIFO order, then rethrows; capture reports `transport_error` and journals `audit_transport_error` |
| Socket reset exception | Same prefix/barrier behavior; saves the exception's string |
| Empty return from `recv()` | Drains the prefix, then reports `remote_close` and journals `connection_closed` |
| WebSocket timeout followed by valid data | Retries the timeout; no permanent failure and no invented frame |
| Disconnect before any frame | Rethrows the same exception with zero received/delivered frames |
| Library socket EOF versus protocol close | websocket-client 1.9.0 maps empty socket reads to the connection-lost exception; a valid close-1000 frame makes its `recv()` return an empty string |

All queued-prefix cases verify source equality, one connection, no reconnect,
no pending ingress, SQLite integrity, zero publications and explicit final
barriers. The capture uses its declared one-connection bound. No tested path
lost a locally received frame or suppressed a transport exception while draining
below the capture's duration/budget stops. These probes do not establish every
possible boundary/race or actual Windows network behavior.

The library probe reproduces the error text with a fake byte stream, without
opening a network socket. It demonstrates a possible origin of the message,
not the actual cause on the PC. Coinbase, an intermediary, local network
conditions and scheduling cannot be distinguished from that text alone.

## What is missing at failure

The reader retains the exception object in memory. After the queue drains,
`capture()` saves its string in `REPORT.json -> attempts[i].error`, with a stop
reason and optional close errors. It does not persist the exception class,
structured errno/winerror, traceback/cause chain, reader-side failure timestamp,
or transport-time queue/writer snapshot. The immediate stderr record added for
overflow is not emitted for ordinary transport loss. Empty stderr here is
consistent with implementation, not evidence that no failure happened.

The journal's final transport barrier has the owner's actual observation/commit
clock, after draining and any intervening work. The tests deliberately delay
the owner and confirm that the barrier timestamp is later than reader failure.
It must not be relabelled as an exact socket-failure time. The `recv()` API also
discards a WebSocket close frame's status/reason in the normal empty-return case.

These are diagnostic limits; no causal timestamp is invented and no runtime
patch is made merely to speculate about this historical disconnect.

## Exact next Windows action: collect existing evidence

Do this before another pilot. Preserve the old attempt and its logs unchanged.
The block below makes a new local review folder, copies only PLAN/REPORT and
small sibling logs, writes hashes/metadata, and queries retained Windows events.
It does not copy the 174MB capture, restart a service, make a network request,
change event-log settings or run another capture. Execute in PowerShell:

```powershell
$base = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE '.moonwalking\analysis\instant_reader_overflow_20261110'
$name = 'asia-20261110-162344'
$attempt = Join-Path $base $name
$out = Join-Path $base ('asia-disconnect-review-' + [DateTime]::UtcNow.ToString('yyyyMMdd-HHmmss-fff'))
if (-not (Test-Path (Join-Path $attempt 'REPORT.json'))) { throw 'Original final REPORT.json is required.' }
New-Item -ItemType Directory $out -ErrorAction Stop | Out-Null
Copy-Item (Join-Path $attempt 'PLAN.json') $out -ErrorAction Stop
Copy-Item (Join-Path $attempt 'REPORT.json') $out -ErrorAction Stop
$report = Get-Content (Join-Path $attempt 'REPORT.json') -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json
$report | Select-Object stop,first_ack_ts_ms,last_reader_receipt_ts_ms,attempts,reader,diagnostic_cohorts | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 12 | Set-Content (Join-Path $out 'FAILURE_CONTEXT.json') -Encoding UTF8
Get-ChildItem $base -File | Where-Object { $_.Name -like "$name-*" -and $_.Extension -in '.txt','.log','.json' } | ForEach-Object { Copy-Item $_.FullName $out -ErrorAction Stop }
Get-ChildItem $base -File | Where-Object { $_.Name -match 'clock|retry|asia' } | Select-Object Name,Length,CreationTimeUtc,LastWriteTimeUtc | ConvertTo-Json | Set-Content (Join-Path $out 'SIBLING_LOG_METADATA.json') -Encoding UTF8
# Last successful frame, not a claimed failure instant; retain a broad interval.
$anchor = [DateTimeOffset]::FromUnixTimeMilliseconds([long]$report.last_reader_receipt_ts_ms)
$start = $anchor.UtcDateTime.AddMinutes(-5).ToLocalTime()
$end = $anchor.UtcDateTime.AddMinutes(15).ToLocalTime()
$logs = @('System','Microsoft-Windows-NetworkProfile/Operational','Microsoft-Windows-WLAN-AutoConfig/Operational','Microsoft-Windows-DNS-Client/Operational')
foreach ($log in $logs) {
    $safe = $log -replace '[/\\]','_'
    try {
        Get-WinEvent -FilterHashtable @{LogName=$log;StartTime=$start;EndTime=$end} -ErrorAction Stop | Select-Object @{Name='TimeUtc';Expression={$_.TimeCreated.ToUniversalTime().ToString('o')}},Id,ProviderName,LevelDisplayName,Message | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 4 | Set-Content (Join-Path $out "$safe.json") -Encoding UTF8
    } catch {
        $_.Exception.Message | Set-Content (Join-Path $out "$safe-unavailable.txt") -Encoding UTF8
    }
}
$hashes = Get-ChildItem $out -File | Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 | Select-Object Path,Hash
$hashes | ConvertTo-Json | Set-Content (Join-Path $out 'HASHES.json') -Encoding UTF8
Write-Output $out
```

Include the following with that folder when continuing on Windows:

1. The original `REPORT.json` attempt fields, especially any
   `socket_close_error`, and the original `${name}-stdout.txt` /
   `${name}-stderr.txt`. Their paths are siblings of the attempt, not inside it.
2. Raw `${name}-clock-before.txt`, `${name}-clock-after.txt`, the separately
   retried clock output (whatever filename was used), and the exact retry time
   and command if the retry was only shown in the terminal. The block lists
   sibling filenames but cannot discover a retry stored elsewhere. Do not
   replace or rename the failed check as a successful check.
3. Retained System/network/WLAN/DNS events around the disconnect and the later
   failed clock check. If the check occurred outside the extraction's 20-minute
   interval, query its actual time separately. Disabled, absent or unreadable
   event logs mean unavailable evidence, not a healthy network.

A socket close/reset, interface disconnect, sleep/resume or driver event near
01:03:48 UTC could explain the transport failure. DNS-client events at the
actual clock-check time could explain that separate failure. A shared interface
outage spanning both would support a link; coincident event times alone do not
prove one. If those events and lower-level socket traces were not retained,
no saved log can reconstruct the exact historical initiating cause. That is an
explicit unresolved result, not a reason to guess or silently repeat the run.
The PowerShell block is provided for Windows and was not executed on Linux.

## Pilot decision and checks

A new PC timing pilot is not yet justified by this review: the tested reader
behavior is correct and the existing local error/events have not been inspected.
The immediate next command collects existing evidence rather than repeating
67 minutes with the same diagnostic limits. The earlier copy-ready pilot
command is historical and is not a recommendation to launch again now.

If local logs remain inconclusive and a future diagnostic capture is separately
chosen, first predeclare measurement of reader-side error type/errno/cause and
UTC/monotonic detection time, any protocol close code/reason, and time-aligned
Windows link/DNS observations. A short startup success alone would not resolve
a failure after 39 minutes. A useful bounded attempt would either complete
warmup and all 20 cohorts with 240 independently verified local observations,
or supply enough failure-time transport/link evidence to distinguish a mechanism.
No reconnect across an interrupted warmup, cap increase, schedule change or
claim of useful timing follows automatically.

Verification uses offline synthetic tests only. The new ten probes are included
in `run_instant_offline_checks.py`, so future saved-attempt checks exercise them.
See [checks](results/instant_reader_disconnect_20261111/CHECKS.json) for commands,
counts and runtime versions. Historical Asia/US evidence, other agent review

===== BEGIN SOURCE 23 | docs/research/INSTANT_BATCHED_SOURCE_PILOT_PROTOCOL_20261108.md | sha256=28cdfc7015985445ded605e8ffe5199068967e49a9c5ebe8e691fac67a4e939e =====
# Batched real-source coverage and latency follow-up

Run one isolated, read-only market-data attempt on the Windows research host.
Use the public Coinbase Exchange socket, BTC-USD, SOL-USD, ALCX-USD and
AURORA-USD, and the same level2_batch, matches, heartbeat and ticker
subscriptions as the original indexed pilot. Use the pre-acknowledgment
book staging amendment. Keep the 10-second quote age and full 30-second
book-span checks.

Select the explicit `batched-256` profile. One socket reader keeps a
256-frame queue and stamps frames before enqueue. The journal takes up to
16 frames that are already queued and preserves FIFO order. It durably
commits input before processing and invokes the diagnostic only after
committing the audit rows. Keep DELETE mode and synchronous EXTRA.
The historical `single-128` profile remains the command default.

## Capture limits

Use one connection, 67 minutes after the writer confirms all
subscriptions, a 249,990-packet soft stop, a 240 MiB logical byte
soft stop and a 1 MiB raw frame limit. The soft byte budget is checked
after each group, subject to the journal's hard 256 MiB record limit.
End the attempt at overflow, transport failure, a budget or the deadline.
Preserve committed records, any recovered pending ingress and the exact
stop reason. Report frames returned by the socket reader and frames
delivered to the writer separately. Coverage probes end at the last
accounted committed reader receipt.

Run a read-only five-sample clock check against time.windows.com before
and after capture. Record its exit status and signed differences.
Use raw signed comparisons for exchange and local wall-clock stamps.
Use the performance counter for local queue and commit durations.
Persist the code commit and selected bounds in the plan before opening
the socket. Keep the prior stopped attempt as separate evidence.

## Cohorts and interpretation

The first of ten consecutive 5s cutoffs is the first grid point at or
after the original acknowledgment receipt plus 62 minutes. At each
cutoff +5s, use the fixed as-of to prepare eight product/direction
diagnostics when the next committed group permits it. Keep unavailable
quotes, baseline inputs and features visible in the report.

The same separate local process polls the SQLite outbox every 25 ms
and records its actual read time for each digest-verified entry.
Report queue wait, receipt-to-audit-commit, cohort preparation,
local outbox commit, observed delivery and missing observations.
Report the 30s quote/trade/book probes at cutoff and cutoff +5s by
product, plus actual warmup and cohort counts.

The repeat addresses the two earlier startup and queue blockers.
Interpret all metrics within this single attempt and record partial
results if a stop prevents warmup. This run measures local research
readiness. The original feed audit and the prospective study freeze
remain separate gates for outcome collection and fitting.

# Batched real-source coverage and latency results

The November 8 attempt at `c9d79be` reached its 67-minute deadline on one
Coinbase Exchange connection. All four products acknowledged at 3:13:17
a.m. Pacific. The last committed reader receipt came 4,019.996 seconds
after acknowledgment. The journal contains **166,035 committed records**,
including the open and close markers. The reader returned 166,035 data
frames, of which the writer committed 166,033. The two remaining frames
are outside the measured prefix at the scheduled stop. The sampled
queue high-water was **97 of 256**, with zero reported overflow and zero
pending ingress records.

The four snapshots arrived after acknowledgment in this attempt. ALCX
received its initial book at sequence 3, 89 ms after acknowledgment.
The pre-acknowledgment path remains verified by the earlier preserved
replay. ALCX quote staleness still limits its usable evidence.

## Coverage

There were 133 probes per product. The table reports the cutoff and
the declared later knowledge time separately.

| Product | Quote at cutoff | Quote by +5s | Full fresh 30s book window by +5s | Trade window by +5s |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| BTC | 130 / 133 | 133 / 133 | 133 / 133 | 133 / 133 |
| SOL | 133 / 133 | 133 / 133 | 133 / 133 | 133 / 133 |
| ALCX | 109 / 133 | 109 / 133 | 47 / 133 | 133 / 133 |
| AURORA | 133 / 133 | 133 / 133 | 132 / 133 | 133 / 133 |

At the cutoff, all 532 trade windows were still awaiting proof. At +5s
they all verified. Ready proof lag p95 ranged from 929 to 936 ms across
products. ALCX's 86 missing full book windows were stale, and AURORA
had one stale span. The side diagnostic paired 11,175 BTC trades,
2,354 SOL trades, five ALCX trades and four AURORA trades. Every paired
ticker and match side was opposite. The thin counts leave the separate
feed-audit gate inconclusive.

## Readiness and local latency

All ten cohorts reached their planned post-warmup cutoffs. Each
product contributed 20 rows across the two directions. BTC and SOL
had both baselines and all five features ready on every row. ALCX
lacked a usable volatility baseline on every row due to stale book
observations. Its F2 was unavailable throughout because its pace
median was nonpositive, and its F3 was ready on 12 of 20 rows.
AURORA had both baselines and F1, F3 and F5 on all 20 rows. Its F2 was
unavailable throughout because its pace median was nonpositive.
Its F4 lacked enough traded candles on all 20 rows.

| Measurement | p95 | Maximum |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Decision preparation, 80 rows | 70.2 ms | 107.1 ms |
| Local outbox commit, 80 rows | 20.2 ms | 26.5 ms |
| Complete eight-row cohort, 10 cohorts | 655.2 ms | 655.2 ms |
| Commit observation to separate local reader, 80 rows | 27 ms | 60 ms |
| Cutoff to separate local reader, 80 rows | 5.601 s | 5.678 s |

The separate reader saw all 80 committed diagnostic rows, with zero
missing or mismatched observations. Cutoff-to-read includes the
predeclared 5s evidence offset. The largest reader-to-writer wait
across recorded frame groups was 686 ms. The largest reader-to-audit
commit time was 763 ms. These timings measure a single run on this
host and delivery to a separate local process.

The startup clock check recorded +1.143 to +1.159 seconds, and the
post-capture check recorded +1.285 to +1.346 seconds. Exchange-stamp
differences retain their signed wall-clock values. Queue and commit
durations use the host performance counter.

The journal hash, SQLite quick check, 166,035 packet records, 166,033
batch processing clocks and all 80 outbox digests and cutoff prefixes
verified. The raw journal stays on the Windows research host. The
portable report, plan, local observations and clock checks are below.
**1,316 targeted checks** passed, with Black, guardrails and 11 frozen
historical hashes verified.

The next decision is to predeclare which combinations of quote freshness,
baseline readiness and trade activity qualify a product for each
feature and for the full study. Keep the thin products in coverage
reporting. This run supplies feasibility evidence for that decision.
The feed audit, decision-age policy and prospective study freeze
remain open before fitting.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 25 | docs/research/INSTANT_REAL_INDEXED_PILOT_RESULTS_20261107.md | sha256=fd839e311dfdf6e9bb15a2e958fecfbf51db7e8eb3c87f3f34bada43ecb4785f =====
# Real indexed latency pilot results

The November 7, 2026 run acknowledged all four products at 10:34:43 PM
Pacific. The last committed reader receipt arrived at 10:55:18 PM.
The run stopped at the 128-frame reader queue limit **20 minutes 35.553
seconds** after acknowledgment, before the 62-minute warmup cutoff.
The reader received 45,955 frames and delivered 45,826. The journal
preserved those frames plus its open and queue-loss markers, for
**45,828 committed records**. The 129 received but undelivered frames
are outside coverage. The ten post-warmup cohorts and local delivery
measurements were unreached.

## Measured prefix

| Product | Fresh cutoff quotes | Full fresh book windows | Trade windows ready at cutoff | Trade windows ready by +5s | p95 proof lag |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| BTC | 39 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 0 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 204 ms |
| SOL | 40 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 0 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 199 ms |
| ALCX | 0 / 40 | 0 / 40 | 0 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 188 ms |
| AURORA | 34 / 40 | 21 / 40 | 0 / 40 | 40 / 40 | 185 ms |

Each product had 1,230 verified strict heartbeat intervals in the
committed prefix. The trade-window check covers the sampled recorded
intervals. Full feed certification requires the separate audit evidence.

For book updates, measured reader-to-writer p95 was 16.1 ms for BTC,
21.4 ms for SOL, 33.9 ms for ALCX, and 21.6 ms for AURORA. The maximum
was 570.2 ms across these groups. Writer-through-commit p95 for book
updates ranged from 8.8 to 14.7 ms, with a maximum of 458.0 ms. This
measures application queue and writer time after frames returned from
the socket reader.

The ending loss marker was recorded 109 ms after the last committed
reader timestamp. Twelve committed data frames shared a 16 ms receipt
span at the tail. The discarded frames have counts without individual
timings, so the journal locates the overflow at the application queue
and leaves its upstream cause unresolved.

## Startup ordering and clock evidence

The ALCX snapshot was committed at sequence 2. The subscription
acknowledgment followed at sequence 3 in the same millisecond. The book
replay only accepts snapshots after acknowledged membership. ALCX sent
no additional snapshot in this segment, leaving it at `awaiting_snapshot`
for all 40 probes. That is a startup ordering failure for this attempt.
AURORA's 19 missing full windows were attributed to stale book spans.

Five read-only startup clock samples reported +891 to +904 ms against
the time source. Five post-run samples reported +920 to +931 ms. The
ending probe began at 11:03:30 PM, about eight minutes after the stop.
Exchange timestamps remain raw signed cross-clock differences. Local
queue and writer durations use the host performance counter.

Next, verify causal handling of snapshots that precede acknowledgment
and build a burst test from the observed arrival pattern before
choosing the next writer and queue policy.

The raw journal is saved on the Windows host under
`%USERPROFILE%\.moonwalking\analysis\instant_real_indexed_pilot_20261107\attempt-01`.
Its SHA-256, all packet digests and sequence, and SQLite quick check
passed. The run and its loss marker are preserved.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 26 | docs/research/INSTANT_STARTUP_AND_BURST_RESULTS_20261108.md | sha256=b4579daf9eaf2f9efc6fc309133bbb9f12b675da4a43c96fd07438ef02caf25f =====
# Startup book and burst verification results

The pre-acknowledgment book amendment activates a staged snapshot on
the first valid subscription response from the same explicitly opened
connection. It keeps the original observation age and requires a
post-acknowledgment heartbeat. Loss, connection change, invalid
book data, and missing membership discard the staged data as specified.

Two new tests reproduced the startup defect on the previous code.
The amended implementation passes both tests plus four rejection
controls. A 271-row replay of the preserved real startup prefix,
using original receipt order and a simulated processing clock,
activated the ALCX snapshot at acknowledgment sequence 3 and
produced a usable quote at the subsequent grid probe. This replay
verifies ordering behavior under the amended code.

## Burst results

Each condition was run three times with the real indexed SQLite writer.
The larger-queue trials delivered their 160-frame bursts in roughly
100–104 ms and drained the complete 205-frame synthetic source,
including startup and the tail. All 12 journals passed ordered-prefix
comparison, single-connection verification and SQLite quick check.

| Queue capacity | Injected writer pause | Outcome | Data frames committed per trial | Peak queue depth | Largest measured queue wait |
| --- | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 128 | 0 ms | 3 / 3 explicit overflows | 14 / 205 | 128 | 280 ms |
| 128 | 450 ms | 3 / 3 explicit overflows | 5 / 205 | 128 | 292 ms |
| 256 | 0 ms | 3 / 3 complete | 205 / 205 | 150 | 1,481 ms |
| 256 | 450 ms | 3 / 3 complete | 205 / 205 | 174 | 1,920 ms |

The larger queue absorbed this workload with the same per-frame
commit path, at the cost of up to about 1.9 seconds in the queue.
The shorter waits at the 128-frame cap describe only the committed
prefix. The experiment uses a declared synthetic arrival schedule
and does not establish the arrival pattern of the discarded live
frames or a safe decision-age budget.

Next, compare bounded transaction batching or another faster writer
against these same conditions, including crash recovery and known-at
availability, before selecting the next real capture profile.

The first synthetic harness attempt stopped during verification
because it compared Python tuples in the generator with JSON lists
on disk. The preserved journal matched after canonicalization.
The complete run used the same declared workload and compared
wire-format JSON. The original live journal and loss marker are
preserved independently.

- [Burst run](results/instant_startup_and_burst_20261108/RUN_02.json)
- [Verification](results/instant_startup_and_burst_20261108/CHECKS.json)

===== BEGIN SOURCE 27 | docs/research/INSTANT_BATCHED_JOURNAL_RESULTS_20261108.md | sha256=adaec120461556f8a9268eb90200c33889508c8c4e9f653632038e8b66dae9e4 =====
# Bounded journal batching results

The bounded writer at `aa953b5` took up to 16 already queued frames at a
time. Both profiles used the same indexed writer fixture and SQLite durability
settings. All 24 journals matched the exact ordered source prefix and passed
SQLite quick check and file-digest verification.

| Queue | Injected pause | Single-frame completions | Batched completions | Worst single receipt through commit | Worst batched receipt through commit |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 128 | 0 ms | 0 / 3 | 1 / 3 | 296 ms | 360 ms |
| 128 | 450 ms | 0 / 3 | 0 / 3 | 337 ms | 322 ms |
| 256 | 0 ms | 3 / 3 | 3 / 3 | 1,540 ms | 396 ms |
| 256 | 450 ms | 3 / 3 | 3 / 3 | 1,988 ms | 857 ms |

Complete runs committed all 205 source frames. In the 128-frame no-pause
case the single writer committed 14 frames in each trial. The batched writer
committed 22, 22 and 205. In the 128-frame pause case each writer committed
the five startup frames. Latency in overflow trials only covers committed
frames. In the complete 256-frame trials, batched worst queue wait was
367 ms without the pause and 829 ms with it. A single batch callback took
as long as 301 ms through the audit commit. The two durable transactions
can add overhead when few frames are waiting.

At a cutoff before ingress commit observation, the indexed evidence
prefix stayed identical to the pre-batch prefix. All three hard exits
recovered the expected prefix. An exit in the audit transaction left two
durable input envelopes and zero processed audit rows from that batch.
An exit during ingress left neither. An exit after audit commit left the
full processed batch. A processing failure preserved the staged inputs
and blocked decision preparation. A malformed frame wrote its barrier
before the connection ended.

**Candidate for the next diagnostic** is a 256-frame reader queue with
up to 16 frames per batch. Predeclare the follow-up at that profile and
measure warmup, quote missingness, frame loss and decision latency on real
data. The stress test establishes neither real coverage nor a decision-age
budget. The real runner still uses its original 128-frame queue and
single-frame writer by default. The stopped capture, feed gate and
historical results retain their prior status.

**1,313 targeted checks passed** on Windows with Python 3.12.10 and SQLite
3.49.1. Black, guardrails and the 11 frozen historical hashes passed.

- [Single-frame run](results/instant_batched_journal_20261108/SINGLE.json)
- [Batched run](results/instant_batched_journal_20261108/BATCHED.json)
- [Checks](results/instant_batched_journal_20261108/CHECKS.json)

# Delayed eligibility and preserved-journal timing comparison

November 9, 2026 (Pacific). This research draft follows the
[eligibility review](INSTANT_ELIGIBILITY_REVIEW_TRIAGE_20261109.md).
It separates evidence readiness from local delivery. The existing directional
reporter and target evaluators retain their original clock and outcome rules.

`instant_delayed_eligibility.assess_cohort` accounts for every declared
product and direction at a cutoff. An absent decision is an explicit
`decision_missing` row. A complete-vector candidate needs all five features,
both baselines and fresh coin/BTC anchor evidence. BTC can have complete
inputs and stays in coverage as the benchmark, outside the non-benchmark
candidate count. The adapter checks the frozen decision's evidence prefix.
It retains component reasons and, when supplied, an observation linked to
that decision's digest with separate local commit and consumer ages.
Being ready and being observed within a diagnostic bound are separate
fields. The caller supplies the planned cutoff after warmup. These are
scoped research observations and do not certify full-feed or study eligibility.

The comparison uses the same ten post-warmup 5s cutoffs, all four products,
and both directions from the November 8 attempt. It uses a scratch copy
of the preserved journal, checks its full digest, reconstructs verified
packets and first-receipt trades, and evaluates fixed knowledge views at
cutoff plus **0, 1.5, 2.5 and 5 seconds**. Market content still stops before
the original cutoff. The journal records full packet start and finish
times. It does not separately record intermediate monitor and book clocks,
so this replay treats both intermediate stages as available at packet
completion. The report flags an as-of inside the next packet's processing.
Replay has no historical delivery timestamps. The original +5s outbox and
independent consumer observations are checked and reported separately.

The second supplied rendering of the Gemini response lists the full 15
hashes, lengths and paths, all matched to the same packet. Its detailed
protocol proposes three bounded 67-minute sessions instead of continuous
collection. Its summary still mentions 24 hours. Treat the bounded sessions
as a follow-up design candidate. Three short attempts can expose timing,
storage and coverage problems across activity periods. They cannot
establish a p99.9 tail or certify the original feed gate. The received
text digest is `4bcde71ae941179a15bdb09114543fd5f57a0d99c11349df3f3c7af8aa3909cd`.
The earlier archived review and its corrections still apply.

## Results

Replay verified all **166,035 packets** and their recorded first-receipt
trade evidence. The +5s replay matched the status, reason and value of
every F1–F5 component in all **80 original outbox decisions**. All 80
consumer records linked to the original decision digest and commit clock.

| Evidence as-of | BTC full inputs | SOL full inputs | ALCX full inputs | AURORA full inputs | Non-benchmark candidate rows | In-flight as-ofs |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| +0ms | 0/20 | 0/20 | 0/20 | 0/20 | 0 | 2/10 |
| +1,500ms | 20/20 | 20/20 | 0/20 | 0/20 | 20 | 2/10 |
| +2,500ms | 20/20 | 20/20 | 0/20 | 0/20 | 20 | 2/10 |
| +5,000ms | 20/20 | 20/20 | 0/20 | 0/20 | 20 | 2/10 |

The +1.5s view preserved the same complete decision-input slots as +5s. The candidate count covers both
directions over ten adjacent cutoffs. It measures availability, not
independent events or ranking quality. The original +5s run observed
a maximum local commit age of **5,662ms** and a maximum consumer age
of **5,678ms**. All 80 original reads were within the illustrative 6s
bound. The other views have no measured delivery ages.

The next step is to predeclare bounded outcome-blind source checks across
activity periods. Test the shorter wait with actual local commit and
consumer clocks and retain the +5s schedule as a reference. Declare a
wider non-benchmark candidate set, as this run still has only SOL with
complete inputs. Keep all monitored products in the denominators.
The feed gate, study freeze and fitting remain closed.

Implementation commit `4b19a7a171c4f1923b85e46f4e33450a263bd51a`. **109 focused tests**, Black 24.8.0
and repository guardrails passed. See the [complete per-slot replay report](results/instant_delayed_eligibility_20261109/REPLAY.json) for counts,
component reasons, source digest and evidence-prefix hashes.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 39 | docs/research/INSTANT_STUDY_FREEZE_CANDIDATE_20261030.md | sha256=ec241844a8fbf28fa8c5159e0d389aa8788ffd89ab88cbfad3267d3f2ef63d1e =====
# Instant study rules: consolidated freeze candidate — 2026-10-30

November 6 addendum: the current 29-file Gemini methodology response is
[archived and reconciled](INSTANT_METHOD_REVIEW_TRIAGE_20261106.md). Retain
the candidate formulas and existing metrics. A two-clock decision interface
must be separately versioned; Sample availability is not receipt-only.
The proposed 2,500ms budget, entry/candle/model/readiness rules are not frozen.
Six synthetic boundary checks pass; no target or runtime implementation changed.

Subsequent implementation: the additive reporting schema and offline sparse
replay/baseline prototype were implemented at `8211262` and independently
reviewed/accepted through `ab95159`; read the
[implementation checkpoint](INSTANT_OFFLINE_PIPELINE_20261030.md).
This does not freeze these study rules or change the target evaluator at
`92fb51d`. The "next implementation" descriptions below retain their draft
history; the independent offline review is complete and real-data verification is next.

**Status: draft for review, not frozen, not permission to fit or deploy.**
Executable labels remain at `92fb51d`; V1 stays the default. Neither executable
contract nor the evaluator changes in this checkpoint. This document combines
[Claude's review](reviews/CLAUDE_INSTANT_V2_REVIEW_20261030.md),
[Codex's acceptance](INSTANT_V2_REVIEW_ACCEPTANCE_20261030.md) and the newly
received [Gemini v2 design review](reviews/GEMINI_V2_DESIGN_REVIEW_20261030.md).
External recommendations are evidence to assess, not user authorization.

Gemini reports inspecting the eight-section `cc3b6a6` packet for `8b5b266` plus
the acceptance report. Its eight stated source hashes match the pinned bytes.
That does not establish that Gemini ran tests or inspected the full patched
`92fb51d` source. The original unamended proposal is still unavailable. Its
claim that every eligible grid row receives a label describes the intended
universe, not a deployed recorder or a measured coverage result.

## 1. Target and reference decisions

Keep `verified_forward` as the primary pointwise outcome, separately at 300s
and 900s, across the causally eligible universe including non-board/no-move
rows. The strict activity/no-retrace target remains a secondary diagnostic.
Neither a forward-window anchor nor anchor minus 30s identifies physical onset.

| Decision | Draft disposition |
|---|---|
| S1: earlier wick | Retain the existing whole-reference-window peak and strict timing semantics. Gemini's narrower search is a different metric, not a fix to a contract violation. Preserve the previous wick; do not overwrite `pre_window_peak_*` with a post-anchor search. |
| Additional continuation diagnostic | A future additive field may report `pre_window_to_forward_peak_consumed_fraction`, using anchor minus 30s as price reference and the maximum over `[anchor,anchor+H]` as denominator. Give it its own name, peak timestamp and null reason. Report both peak references and whether the whole-window peak predates the anchor. This is not physical-move consumption, a competing fitted target, or a reason to choose whichever result looks better. |
| S2: baseline cutoffs | Retain sigma ending at `floor((anchor-35000)/60000)*60000` and pace ending at `anchor-30000`. Both are causal. The completed-minute volatility sample and nonoverlapping activity baseline estimate different quantities. Verify the actual baseline builder, not just supplied metadata. |
| S3: missing fraction reason | Recommend an additive `pre_window_consumed_fraction_reason` field: source reason when reference quality fails (with an explicit fallback reason), `invalid_pre_window_peak` for a missing/nonfinite peak, `nonpositive_pre_window_peak` for a finite peak <=0, and null for a populated fraction. Preserve unavailable outcomes as null. The reporting contract/implementation still need this amendment and tests before freeze. |

Why retain S1? On the reproduced path the earlier wick is 0.04 log units, the
trigger is 0.002 and the later peak is 0.022 from the pre-window reference.
The existing fraction is 5%; Gemini's alternative is 9.09%. Both are valid
answers to differently named questions. The existing strict result is late
because the whole-window peak has passed, not because the fraction exceeds
50%. Report the separate conditions; do not turn this strict diagnostic into
a claim that most of a physical move has elapsed. The primary forward label
does not require that strict timing classification.

Do not clamp negative or greater-than-one fractions to `[0,1]`; those values
can describe a reversal or an observation outside the denominator's interval.
Any changed semantics require a separately versioned contract before fitting.

## 2. Alert success and event coverage answer different questions

For an actual publication at `t`, retain its recorded 5s decision cutoff `b`,
publication delay, input availability and quote age. Evaluate the primary
pointwise `verified_forward(b,H)` on its declared fixed horizon. This is a
decision-cutoff outcome, not a fill at a price from before publication. Keep
illustrative spread/fee outcomes distinct from execution claims.

For complete mature labels:

```text
pointwise_success_H = verified_forward(b,H) is True
pointwise_failure_H = verified_forward(b,H) is False
ungradable_H = pending or unavailable
```

Do not erase a pointwise failure because another catalog window is associated
with the alert. Keep ungradable counts and coverage visible, outside both
success and failure counts. Evaluate the two horizons in separate tables.

Gemini's proposed false-alert rule is not interchangeable with this rule:

| Synthetic alert | Its own target | Gemini's broad association rule |
|---|---|---|
| 5m alert; move begins at +340s | False: move starts after +300s | Forgiven by a qualifying catalog window anchored at +65s |
| 15m alert; move begins at +940s | False: move starts after +900s | Also forgiven by a window anchored at +65s |
| 5m alert at an earlier move's endpoint | False for the next 5m | Forgiven by the old window's inclusive association endpoint |

These are not demonstrations of live-feature leakage. They demonstrate a
different success deadline and association meaning. A broad retrospective
association may be reported as coverage; it must not replace a failed
fixed-horizon prediction or be advertised as physical early-warning success.

Proposed separate **forward-window coverage diagnostic**, pending an
implementation/matching review: group by product, direction and horizon;
sort windows by anchor and alerts by publication; assign the earliest unused
qualifying alert to each window in chronological order, allowing an edge only
in `[anchor-180s, forward_peak]`. One alert covers at most one window per
horizon; an identical timestamp is ordered by the retained publication ID.
An alert can have separate associations in 5m and 15m tables. Retain unmatched
alerts/windows. This rule is a transparent candidate, not implemented or a
physical-event matching protocol.

Call its missing coverage `uncovered_forward_window_rate`, not physical
`missed_move_rate`. Keep operational missed-move/false-alert rates disabled
until the entire association/exposure contract is approved and executable.

Exposure rules for the eventual protocol must include mature trailing horizons,
warmup, verified health, feed/universe membership and candidate eligibility.
Report healthy board wall-clock hours, eligible-product hours, universe sizes
and ungradable alerts; a shrinking universe must remain visible. Compare all
rankers on identical complete-data rows as well as their natural coverage.

## 3. Five-feature candidate, fixed before outcome inspection

These are proposed formulas, not implemented features or empirically selected
weights. Keep the fixed 30s/180s windows; do not optimize a window grid on the
discovery sample. For direction `d=+1/-1`, use causal midpoint log returns,
the declared sigma floor, verified match sides and receipt-time windows.
The following makes one concrete candidate available for implementation
review; changing it later must be recorded before any discovery outcomes.

| Feature | Proposed single fitted input | Required comparison |
|---|---|---|
| F1 acceleration | `d * (r_coin_30s - r_coin_prior150s/5) / sigma_1m` using adjacent nonoverlapping endpoint returns | Current velocity and market-relative movement; verify incremental value beyond their levels |
| F2 signed participation | `d * ((buy_usd-sell_usd)/(buy_usd+sell_usd)) * log1p(volume_pace)`; volume pace is current 30s match USD divided by the median of 120 completed, nonoverlapping 30s windows ending at `b-30s` | Unsigned pace/count and the full model without F2 |
| F3 sampled book pressure | `d/30s * integral((bid_size-ask_size)/(bid_size+ask_size), b-30s..b)` over last-observed BBO state | Spread-only control plus F2; spread stays separately recorded rather than hidden in a second F3 predictor |
| F4 active compression/expansion | `compression * max(d*r_coin_30s/sigma_1m,0)`, where `compression=1/(1+log(max(high)/min(low))/(sigma_1m*sqrt(15)))` over traded closed candles in the prior 15 completed minutes | Sigma/F1 and the same candidate without F4 |
| F5 market relative | `d * (r_coin_180s - beta*r_BTC_180s) / sigma_1m`, with synchronized endpoints and beta already available at the decision cutoff | Raw coin movement, beta-fallback/BTC-regime strata and coin returns under stated cost scenarios |

For F2, require >=5 unique matches and positive measured baselines/denominators;
do not insert epsilon/neutral scores to manufacture availability. The feature
uses volume pace; the secondary activity trigger's count-or-volume maximum
remains a different quantity. Record every excluded grid row and its reason.
Raw match windows are `[b-30s,b)`, and the 120 baseline windows end at `b-30s`.

For F3, use receipt-ordered observation times and require valid, <=10s-old
quotes throughout the 30s integration interval. An unchanged carried quote
keeps its original age. A stale/unknown duration makes the primary feature
unavailable; do not remove that duration and renormalize the rest to 30s.
This is sampled L1 information, not a continuous full-book measurement.

For F4, require >=10 traded, closed, already-available candles in the prior
15 minutes and verified health for any confirmed empty interval. Never invent
OHLC for an absent/unverified candle. No compression claim follows from an
outage. The explicit directional expansion interaction keeps this one input
from rewarding quietness alone on both boards.

Unnormalized decayed sums at fixed 30s/180s half-lives remain separately logged
diagnostics under the corrected math. This candidate chooses boxcar endpoint
F1/F5 as fitted inputs; do not add both decay states as extra fitted weights,
or select a smoother variant using validation. That choice is declared here
as a new draft recommendation, not something Gemini has already endorsed.
For those incremental decay diagnostics, beta must be known before each
increment; do not rebuild past states with a newly estimated beta. In contrast,
the proposed endpoint F5 is computed at its own cutoff with an already-known
beta. Keep that distinction and target-shared scale/beta controls visible.

Support means prespecified meaningful incremental value beyond the comparator,
with matched coverage/false-alert burden, ablations and paired uncertainty.
Robust harm argues against the declared mechanism. A point estimate near zero
or interval spanning zero is inconclusive unless the interval also rules out
the separately prespecified meaningful benefit. Probability metrics require
a declared probability model; this candidate currently supplies ordinal ranks.

## 4. Gates and next implementation step

The fit remains a separately fitted instant model for each direction, sharing
raw market data with the broader outlook, not its formula/weights. No numeric
weights, readiness minima or stage boundaries are invented by this document.
The existing hard extension gate stays; remaining stage rules, regularization,
scaling and meaningful-effect/power criteria must be fixed before fitting.

The feed gate remains false. Gemini's suggestion to restrict to passing
products is a possible future amended universe, not a pass of the current
all-product gate. Any bounded supplemental diagnostic must be predeclared;
do not repeat completed samples until a favorable verdict appears. Preserve
all failures and the original thin-product verdict.

Next concrete implementation work: the additive diagnostic/schema amendment,
then a standalone offline 5s recorder/replay and causal baseline-builder
contract with healthy-zero versus outage, receipt boundaries, deduplication,
quote ages, late recovery, 60 closed returns and 120 pace-window tests. Do not
attach it to the always-on collector or change live rankings during this step.
This checkpoint does not implement that recorder or launch another capture.

After the data gates pass, collect at least 14 valid shadow days, with fixed
discovery/validation split, 60m phase purge and all labels matured. Freeze the
code/model/universe before fresh validation once. Use paired UTC-day blocks
retaining all products/rankers together; report independent episode/regime
coverage and the fragility of seven validation days. Insufficient readiness
requires predeclared additional collection, not relaxed minima or reused
validation. Physical-move recall remains unavailable until its own event
definition is specified.

## Reproduction and scope

Run `python scripts/scoring_experiment/reproduce_instant_study_choices.py`.
The [saved synthetic evidence](results/instant_study_choices_20261030/REPRODUCTIONS.json)
records the current evaluator hash, wick/reference alternatives, two delayed
move cases, the completed-move case and the missing diagnostic. All are
fabricated paths for checking definitions, not market performance.
The [Gemini archive manifest](reviews/GEMINI_V2_DESIGN_REVIEW_20261030_MANIFEST.json)
records source availability and normalization. The recorded 316 implementation
checks remain from `92fb51d`; this document does not claim a fresh full-suite
pass. Historical experiments, executable targets, source packets, production
and other agents' worktrees are unchanged.

Current [draft verification](results/instant_study_choices_20261030/CHECKS.json):
identical synthetic replay, all eight packet sections/archive hashes, all 11
historical hashes, unchanged evaluator/contract bytes, Black 24.8.0, repository

documented examples and checkpoint integrity, not the candidate's predictive
value or power.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 40 | docs/research/INSTANT_METHOD_REVIEW_TRIAGE_20261106.md | sha256=eba23a69dbf4528c9dfcde04f6a3a86d80d8024080f35553e0b04f3b0f4b5a04 =====
# Current Gemini methodology review: dispositions and next implementation

November 6, 2026 (Pacific). The new [29-file response](reviews/GEMINI_METHOD_REVIEW_20261106.md)
is received and reconciled against packet `fee55a7`. Read its
[archive manifest](reviews/GEMINI_METHOD_REVIEW_20261106_MANIFEST.json).
This is distinct from the already reconciled October 30 eight-file response.
All 29 supplied path/hash entries contain the expected packet metadata;
Source 07 also contains a shortened erroneous token, preserved verbatim with
its correct explicitly supplied hash. This is metadata comparison, not proof
of Gemini's independent hash computation. Gemini reports no test execution or
empirical performance verification. The original unamended proposal remains absent.

**Disposition:** retain the existing target/diagnostic architecture and the
five fixed candidate formulas. Accept the two-clock issue as the next design
dependency, with the corrections below. Do not freeze new numerical limits,
change an existing availability field, or enable fitting/collection/live use.
The [synthetic checks](results/instant_method_review_20261106/CHECKS.json)
test contract boundaries, not predictive benefit.

## Accepted and already implemented

- Keep V1 default and opt-in V2 `verified_forward(b,H)` as the fixed-cutoff
  pointwise research outcome at 300s and 900s. It measures future displacement
  and retention; a high hit rate alone does not establish early discovery,
  physical onset or an executable return from publication.
- Keep whole-reference-window strict timing, its separately named forward-peak
  fraction and explicit null/left-censor reasons. Do not reopen the old S1
  substitution or let associated catalog coverage forgive pointwise failure.
- Keep deterministic earliest-unused association, own-direction gradability,
  per-board time unions, paired ranker comparisons and visible natural coverage.
- Preserve the input ledger's actual completion times and epoch attribution.
  Stage 1 is intentionally incapable of positive study eligibility; the review
  does not convert scoped ID evidence into full health.
- Preserve fixed 30s/180s input windows and existing sigma/pace cutoffs. Sigma,
  beta and spread are shared feature/target controls, so ablations and stratum
  reporting are necessary. Models and weights remain separate from broader
  short/medium/long outlook scoring.

## Required corrections to the response

### C1. Availability cannot be redefined as receipt-only

The proposed separation of feature content cutoff `b` from later proof and
decision completion is useful. Its explanation of the existing `Sample` field
is incorrect: `instant_targets.TargetEvaluator._quality` rejects
`row.available_ts_ms > row.ts_ms` as `future_receipt`, even when grading occurs
much later. `_baseline_quality` also rejects a baseline/beta unavailable at
the anchor. `instant_directional_exposure._slots` requires eligibility
availability at or before its cutoff. `PublicationTiming` represents later
proofs, but explicitly marks legacy cutoff compatibility false and never
enables fitting. Chronology validation is not a semantic bridge into either API.

Four synthetic V1/V2 × up/down cases confirm that an otherwise ready label
becomes unavailable when the anchor input is known 180ms late. Another case
confirms that a valid publication timeline does not make the same late
eligibility record acceptable to the existing directional reporter.

**Next design:** a separately versioned timed-component/decision contract must
retain observation cutoff, evidence knowledge, computation completion and
publication as separate clocks. Observations received at/after `b` cannot enter
its features, although later evidence may verify a prior receipt interval.
Do not move late trades into earlier volume buckets or rewrite frozen cohorts.
Existing cutoff inputs and baseline provenance stay intact. Later grading and
any publication-anchored entry/outcome require explicit adapters; existing
`evaluate(..., asof=later)` alone does not grant the proposed semantics.

### C2. 2,500ms is a proposal, not a measured sufficient budget

The preserved hour's 965.5ms median / 1,993ms maximum is additional receipt wait
for the earliest interval in each merge. It excludes a general guarantee about
boundary-heartbeat phase, computation, queues, serialization and publication.
For example, a hypothetical 1,000ms boundary wait plus 1,993ms additional merge
wait already totals 2,993ms. The sixth synthetic case records that chronology
and correctly fails a supplied 2,500ms diagnostic bound. This does not estimate
its real frequency or select a different cap.

Defer a numerical budget until a predeclared data-only source/latency protocol
specifies measurement, busy-period/missingness strata and allowed exclusions.
A policy may reject late rows; it must report them, not guarantee every row
publishes within the budget. Engineering safety bounds, a pilot diagnostic
criterion and a frozen study age limit are different decisions.

### C3. Publication diagnostics need actual quotes and return units

`floor(published/5000)*5000` identifies a grid boundary, not the last quote.
Use actual receipt and processing timestamps plus age. A first post-publication
quote can provide a separately named delayed quote benchmark; it is not an
executed fill or the price known at publication. Its delay/selection rule and
maximum wait must be declared, including how missing or stale entries are counted.

The response's bare `mid_after - mid_at_b` has price units, not basis points.
For a mid-price diagnostic, declare a normalized, direction-signed simple or
log return and multiply by 10,000. A side-aware execution-cost scenario needs
declared bid/ask entry/exit rules, fees and limitations; L1 observations do not
prove a fill, depth, impact or executable profit. If entry is delayed to `t_e`,
an outcome ending at `t_e+H` has a different maturity from `published+H` and
`b+H`. Keep all named estimands separate.

### C4. Unmeasured sources and new gates remain proposals

The pinned hour measured ticker-derived endpoint freshness, not a real
`level2_batch` capture. Its thin-product staleness cannot be presented as a
measurement of both channels. Sampled carried-book integration also does not
establish continuous physical book validity or intrabatch paths.

F4's suggestion to align candle end with sigma's earlier endpoint is a policy
choice, not an implemented requirement. The draft says prior closed candles;
freeze candle source, half-open intervals, alignment and completion before use.
Receipt-built OHLC must come from actual trades, with verified empty intervals
kept empty. Merely using the same store does not prove causal availability.
Similarly, a new F1 minimum-trade gate, liquidity thresholds, a 5-product/100-event/
50-alert minimum, stage thresholds, ±5 clipping, nonnegative weights and model/
regularization/calibration choices have not been adopted. Do not silently add
them to the current four-product diagnostic or change the original failed gate.

## F1–F5 implementation/evaluation guardrails

The exact formulas remain those in section 3 of the
[consolidated candidate](INSTANT_STUDY_FREEZE_CANDIDATE_20261030.md). These are
mechanism hypotheses, not evidence of informed trading, catalysts or spoofing.

| Feature | Required comparison and evidence |
|---|---|
| F1 acceleration | Compare against current 30s velocity plus F5 on synchronized midpoint endpoints. Test incremental value of rate change, not velocity level; preserve sigma-floor and extension strata. |
| F2 signed participation | Compare against unsigned USD pace and counts, with fixed count/positive-denominator requirements and explicit unknown-side availability. Signed flow must add benefit beyond activity on the same causal rows; the response's F2 table omits a full coverage-control cell, so retain these controls explicitly. |
| F3 sampled pressure | Compare the full 30s receipt-state integral against spread plus F2. Carry original observation ages; any stale/unknown positive-duration segment makes it unavailable, with no shortened-window renormalization. Record latency and source identity separately from the feature value. |
| F4 active compression/expansion | Compare against the expansion interaction alone plus F1/sigma. Keep real traded-candle counts and outage exclusions; a sparse or empty book is not evidence of compression. Candle alignment remains to be declared. |
| F5 market relative | Compare against raw signed 180s coin return, with beta fallback/BTC-regime controls. Report residual-label performance and separately named raw/net cost-scenario returns so matching a residual-defined target is not mistaken for execution benefit. |

For every feature, support requires prespecified meaningful incremental benefit
with matched coverage/burden and paired uncertainty. Robust harm can reject the
declared mechanism; an upper bound below a prespecified meaningful benefit can
reject that benefit. A zero/negative point estimate alone or an interval spanning
both zero and meaningful benefit remains inconclusive. Do not choose whichever
of IC, Top-K, horizon, direction or subgroup looks best after validation.

## Freeze order and immediate next implementation

1. Implement timed baseline/component evidence under separate provenance and
   completion clocks. Preserve 60 closed minute returns and 120 completed
   nonoverlapping pace windows. Never infer warmup from elapsed time or relabel
   a late baseline as known at `b`. This implementation work can proceed without
   choosing weights or a numerical publication budget.
2. Specify the new delayed-decision/grading interfaces, F3 piecewise-constant
   boundaries and F4 candle source/alignment before connecting positive eligibility.
   The present ledger and directional report retain their existing semantics.
3. Before any new source pilot, predeclare the sources/universe, full-health
   evidence, real connection IDs, streaming/storage/durability profile and latency
   diagnostic criteria. Preserve the original four-product verdict; no new capture
   is authorized by this review.
4. Before an outcome study, freeze feature/missingness/stage rules, ranker roster,
   four direction/horizon tables, alert cooldown, exposure/burden estimands,
   discovery split/purge and uncertainty. Future outcome missingness cannot shrink
   decision exposure. Report both ungradable burden and its uncertainty; failure
   count divided by board hours is a descriptive observed burden, not missingness-
   adjusted operational performance when many alerts remain ungradable.
5. Before fitting/fresh validation, freeze a primary effect/comparator, model and
   readiness/power rules. Fourteen valid shadow days are a starting collection
   protocol, not demonstrated power for five marginal effects. Any extension
   schedule, maximum duration and stopping rule must be predeclared; do not inspect
   held-out performance repeatedly or extend until a favorable result appears.

This checkpoint archives/reconciles methodology and adds six synthetic boundary
checks. No executable contract, evaluator, recorder, feature/model, numeric policy,
historical result or production behavior changes. The study remains unfrozen,
physical onset undefined, the feed gate inconclusive, and fitting/live use off.

# Gemini eligibility review: dispositions after the batched source pilot

November 9, 2026 (Pacific). The [Gemini response](reviews/GEMINI_ELIGIBILITY_REVIEW_20261109.md)
is reconciled against the 15-source packet at `17b87ac` and the real-source
attempt at implementation `c9d79be`. All 15 listed paths, lengths and
shortened digests match the packet manifest. Gemini reports reading all
sections and running no tests or independent digest calculations. Some pasted
table cells end mid-sentence. The [archive manifest](reviews/GEMINI_ELIGIBILITY_REVIEW_20261109_MANIFEST.json)
identifies the received bytes and the preserved text.

## Adopt as design directions

- Keep the declared product × cutoff × direction inventory and report every
  planned slot, including ALCX and AURORA. Enforce that completeness against
  the independently declared plan in the producer. The existing directional
  reporter can reject a one-sided omission but cannot detect a product/cutoff
  omitted from both boards.
- Use all five ready features and both ready baseline components for a
  complete-vector candidate. Keep each feature's status and missingness reason.
  Verified empty windows and a ready zero are distinct from unavailable
  evidence. A ready pace baseline can have a zero median and still make F2
  unavailable. Continue using the calculated median: 60 positive-volume bins
  and 60 proved zero bins already yield a positive median. Preserve beta
  fallback as a separate flag. Keep the existing formulas for the next
  engineering check.
- Permit feature-specific coverage diagnostics with declared inputs and
  controls. Report the natural coverage of each diagnostic and the
  intersection for matched comparisons. Keep a single ranker's readiness
  separate from shared market evidence, eventual outcome gradability and
  actual delivery. The current delayed grader requires all five features;
  a ready partial vector does not yet have a grade or an evaluated ranker.
- Give a versioned delayed eligibility adapter separate fields for content
  cutoff, evidence as-of, feature completion, preparation completion, local
  commit and consumer observation. Keep cutoff `b` on the 5s grid. Both the
  book lookup and indexed lookup select market receipts strictly before
  (b); a later proof or payload completion may verify earlier receipts
  without extending the window. Preserve the existing reporter's
  `available_ts_ms <= ts_ms` rule and the original decision record.
- Preserve the difference between an input that missed its evidence as-of,
  a prepared decision that missed its delivery deadline, and evidence that
  contradicts an already observed decision during later grading. Keep all
  three in the accounting. For a published decision, a later contradiction
  can make the outcome ungradable without rewriting the decision or shrinking
  exposure. Keep per-board time unions and own-direction 300s/900s grades
  separate from planned-slot coverage and product-hours.

## Corrections and evidence limits

**BTC is the reference in the current delayed V2 outcome.** The timed
baseline estimates BTC beta against BTC as 1, including the flat-benchmark
fallback. Its F5 value and signed forward residual then cancel by construction.
The V2 minimum terminal move is positive. A synthetic rising BTC price path
confirmed a zero residual and no positive V2 grade in both directions at both
horizons, with and without the beta fallback flag. Treat BTC as a benchmark and
engineering control in the proposed study roster, keeping its rows in the
readiness report. A study of BTC moves would need a separate target definition.
SOL was the only non-benchmark product with all five features ready across
the sampled cohorts. One eligible non-benchmark product at a cutoff cannot
provide a cross-sectional comparison. Declare a wider non-benchmark candidate
universe through an outcome-blind coverage rule before prospective selection.

**The timing alternatives are proposals.** In the 532 sampled 30s trade
windows, zero were verified at cutoff and all were verified in the +5s view.
The largest recorded ready-proof delay in that later view was **1,003ms**.
For the 40 complete BTC/SOL rows, all recorded feature availability times
were at most **897ms** after cutoff. These are observations made from the
saved +5s view. They do not establish readiness at +1.5s for the whole cohort
or show what would have been delivered by +2.5s. The run actually used a
fixed +5s evidence offset. Across all 80 rows, the last local commit was
at +5,662ms and the last separate consumer read at +5,678ms. A +6s bound
contains those measurements in this run. It is neither a tested deadline
policy nor a measure of public delivery.

**Counts describe readiness and measurement.** Of the 20 rows per product,
BTC and SOL each had 20 complete feature vectors. ALCX had 12 ready F3
observations and no complete vectors. ALCX's F5 reason was
`stale_book_observation` on six rows, including both directions at cutoff
`1794140150000`, and `volatility_baseline_unavailable` on 14. The feature
reason by itself does not identify which quote endpoint was stale or whether
a separate cutoff spread control was ready. AURORA had 20 ready rows each
for F1, F3 and F5, with F2 blocked by the pace median and F4 blocked by the
traded-candle minimum. No one scored or graded these rows in the pilot.
The ten 5s diagnostic cohorts start at `1794140120000`; the
`1794140130000` in the coverage summary is the first eligible **30s
probe grid** point. Both use the same acknowledgment-based warmup boundary.

**Keep feed certification separate.** The paired side counts and sampled
receipt-ID proofs are valuable evidence for the run. The original gate
also requires REST reconciliation, adequate representation of each maker
side and its other declared checks. Gemini's 100-pair proposal, the 25%
product duty cycle and the proposed tail/coverage percentages stay open.
Record actual overflow events and incomplete attempts, rather than
inferring overflow from a sampled queue watermark. The before/after
host-clock offsets do not establish a rate that can be extrapolated to
another session.

## Next engineering check

1. Implement the research-only delayed eligibility adapter with complete
   inventory accounting, separate readiness and deadline fields, and a
   regression check that late evidence or a later grade cannot rewrite the
   frozen decision. Preserve the existing target, feed gate and reporter
   contracts. Distinguish the BTC benchmark role from the proposed
   non-benchmark ranker cohort.
2. First compare fixed evidence prefixes at +0, +1.5, +2.5 and +5 seconds
   on the preserved journal without grading outcomes. Report readiness
   and its failure reasons at each as-of. A replay can test visibility
   and reproducibility; it cannot establish live scheduling or delivery
   at an earlier offset.
3. Use that comparison to predeclare a bounded, outcome-blind capture
   across distinct activity periods. Declare the non-benchmark candidate
   set, a warmup and attempt-stop/retry rule, the tested timing schedules
   and the required scope of side/coverage evidence in advance. Observe
   local commit and consumer delay directly. Keep partial sessions and
   missing slots in the report. A small number of adjacent cohorts can
   find blockers; it cannot certify a p99.9 latency tail or 99.5% uptime.
   A longer run would need a separately verified storage/rotation plan.
4. Resolve full feed certification, publication age and prospective
   sample design separately before authorizing outcome collection. Freeze
   model, comparison, burden, sample, purge and stopping rules before
   fitting and fresh validation. The existing outcome and historical
   validation locks still apply.

The [pilot result](INSTANT_BATCHED_SOURCE_PILOT_RESULTS_20261108.md) and
