===== BEGIN SOURCE 42 | docs/research/INSTANT_COVERAGE_AND_QUOTE_POLICY_20261030.md | sha256=2bd6f0170642223a6fecca45436b69bae2655dfc0f88a3b63ddcc19f619e60a4 =====
# Offline coverage monitor and next quote-source policy

October 30, 2026. Implementation specification; **not a frozen study or a live
feed certification**. This advances the blockers measured in the
[preserved replay](INSTANT_CAPTURE_REPLAY_RESULTS_20261030.md). It does not
reopen the locked historical comparison or evaluate F1-F5 outcomes.

## What is implemented

`scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_coverage.py` processes saved public
WebSocket envelopes, with no sockets, backend imports or account access.
The new [monitor contract](INSTANT_COVERAGE_CONTRACT_V1_DRAFT.json) is separate
from the existing recorder and target contracts.

- Subscription membership is acknowledged per product and connection.
  Disconnects, errors, queue-overflow markers and lost required subscriptions
  invalidate continuity. A new acknowledgement and heartbeat baseline are
  required before a fresh interval can pass.
- Consecutive heartbeat watermarks define an expected set of trade IDs.
  It must equal the unique live-match IDs received in the half-open receipt
  interval. Missing IDs, unexpected IDs, regressing watermarks, gaps above
  5 seconds and ID spans above 100,000 fail that interval. Boundary collisions
  are conservative failures, not reassigned executions.
- Identity is product plus trade ID, first receipt wins. Decimal amounts are
  compared exactly for duplicate conflicts. Later conflicting duplicates veto
  overlapping evidence only when the conflict is known. REST, `last_match`
  and ticker sizes cannot repair live receipt counts.
- Local logger sequence must be contiguous; receipt and monotonic clocks
  cannot regress. Missing monotonic metadata or invalid amounts fail the
  artifact closed. At most 100,000 packets and 32 declared products are
  accepted; a truncated prefix is never called a complete input.
- Evidence availability uses the measured **completion** of processing and
  at least receipt plus 1ms. A message received exactly at a cutoff is outside
  observations strictly before that cutoff. CLI replay uses the current clock,
  never the historical receipt time. The injectable clock is for testing and
  remains a trusted assertion, not authenticated historical evidence.

`assess(product, start, end, asof=...)` keeps `receipt_id_check_passed`,
`transport_observed_ok` and quote quality separate. A passing ID check is
conditional on the recorded heartbeat range and receipt attribution. It
does **not** establish exchange-event completeness before a lagging watermark,
independent REST agreement, upstream queue latency, durable consumer writes,
or unobserved book continuity. `full_feed_verified` and quote verification
remain false. Proofs are intentionally **not** a `ReplayStore.add_health`
drop-in. The legacy writer's omitted source tag is interpreted as WebSocket;
a reshaped recovery must retain an explicit non-WebSocket source. Shape and
hash metadata do not authenticate provenance.

The bounded CLI creates a new output directory outside the input directory,
hashes the unchanged input before/after and writes a report plus ID-check
records. Failures retain `FAILURE.json`; existing output is refused.

```sh
python scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_coverage.py ~/.moonwalking/analysis/feed_audit_campaign_20261028/attempt-01/websocket.jsonl.gz --products BTC-USD SOL-USD ALCX-USD AURORA-USD --out-dir ~/.moonwalking/analysis/coverage-check-new
```

The [paired-sample adapter](INSTANT_PAIRED_SAMPLE_CONTRACT_V1_DRAFT.json),
`scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_samples.py`, pairs the existing recorder's
coin and BTC snapshots at one cutoff. It exposes no later-asof override.
Both prices and coverage must qualify then. The immutable result contains a
target `Sample` or explicit missing reasons with `sample=None`. Genuine
verified empty windows stay zero. Quote receipt age, exact bucket-start trade
counts, input/producer provenance and separate signed-flow availability are
preserved. Unknown trade side does not turn otherwise valid target prices
and counts into an F2 observation. This adapter uses the recorder's existing
trusted health inputs; it neither strengthens them nor supports the new
book source yet. The target evaluator and V1/V2 JSON bytes stay unchanged.

## Amendment 1: bounded merged reconciliation (November 3, 2026)

The strict per-interval rule above stays the first check, and its failed records
are kept. On the preserved hour, all 33 failed proofs were heartbeat/match
ordering races with no ID lost, yet BTC's vetoes alone would have left 41% of
5-minute and 3% of 15-minute anchors clean, clustered in busy tape. See the
[review](reviews/CLAUDE_INSTANT_COVERAGE_REVIEW_20261103.md). So adjacent failed
proofs may be re-checked as one span, within explicit limits:

| Limit | Value | Reason |
|---|---:|---|
| Proofs in one merged span | **4** | One race spans two proofs; this allows two consecutive races |
| Merged span duration | **10,000ms** | Bounds the added proof latency; normal heartbeat spacing is about 1s |
| Merged trade-ID span | **100,000** | The existing per-pair bound, applied to the whole span |
| Mergeable failures | `missing_live_match_ids`, `receipt_ids_outside_watermarks` only | Only these can be an ordering race |

- **Same rule, larger span.** The expected IDs must exactly equal the unique IDs
  received on that connection. A truly lost or unexpected ID still fails.
- **Never merged:** heartbeat gaps, watermark regressions, ID-span failures,
  verified intervals, a different connection, or any break in receipt-time or
  watermark continuity. A known conflicting duplicate still vetoes the span.
- **Continuity barriers (correction, November 4, 2026).** Each product carries a
  continuity epoch, advanced in receipt-sequence order at a connection change, an
  audit, transport or queue failure, and a lost or re-added required subscription.
  No merged proof, `assess` chain or interval trade count crosses an epoch, even
  when every event shares one millisecond and recovery reuses the connection ID.
  Codex found the false pass this closes in
  `docs/research/RECONCILIATION_BOUNDARY_REVIEW_20261104.md` on
  `codex/instant-radar-20261028`. Intervals on either side still verify and
  reconcile on their own. A healthy repeated acknowledgement is not a barrier. A
  trade received before a barrier at the barrier's millisecond is not counted after
  it, which can only add a veto.
- **Merges stay disjoint.** A run ends at an earlier merged proof, so a race nested
  around an already merged pair stays vetoed even though all its IDs arrived. This
  is a deliberate false veto, chosen so `assess` never handles overlapping proofs.
  It did not occur on the preserved hour. Measure it in the next pilot.
- **No attribution change.** Trades keep their first receipt time, so bucket counts
  do not move. This supersedes the earlier statement only in that a boundary race
  may later be repaired; executions are still never reassigned.
- **No backdating.** A merged proof is known at the closing heartbeat's processing
  completion. Until then the failed interval still fails. Certifying a bucket can
  therefore take longer, which the publication-timing draft must account for.
- **Limits were chosen after seeing coverage from that one hour** (trade IDs, receipt
  times and counts; no prices, returns or labels) and are not fitted to outcomes.
  Check them against the next pilot's run lengths; change them only by a new
  versioned amendment made before reading outcomes.

**Replay of the preserved hour:** all 33 failed proofs reconciled into 16 merged
proofs, none unreconciled; the 14,363 strict proofs are unchanged. The largest merge
used 3 proofs and spanned 2,990ms, so no limit was reached. The earliest repaired
interval in each of the 16 merges waited a median of 965.5ms longer (maximum
1,993ms), measured between receipt boundaries. Codex independently verified
these counts and accepted the continuity correction; see
[the acceptance](INSTANT_RECONCILIATION_ACCEPTANCE_20261104.md). This is one
hour, not a rate estimate or live processing-latency measurement, and no quote
or label availability is implied.

The monitor remains offline. Quote coverage, the feed gate and live eligibility are
unchanged, and it is still not wired into `Sample` coverage.

## Quote-source choice for the next implementation

Use **Coinbase Exchange `level2_batch`**, initially alongside `ticker`,
`matches` and `heartbeat` on the same public feed profile. This is a declared
new source candidate, not a replacement for the old audit evidence. Keep all
four pilot products and the original thin-side gate.

Coinbase documents ticker batching on matches, possible drops from `matches`,
and heartbeat trade IDs for detecting gaps. Level 2 starts with a full book
snapshot, then sends absolute price-level sizes; zero deletes a level.
`level2_batch` uses the same schema in 50ms batches, without authentication.
Its timestamp refers to the latest event in the batch.
[Official channel reference](https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/exchange/websocket-feed/channels).
Coinbase also recommends batch channels and distributing heavy subscriptions
across connections. [Official feed practices](https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/exchange/websocket-feed/best-practices).
The public Exchange endpoint is `wss://ws-feed.exchange.coinbase.com`.
[Official overview](https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/exchange/websocket-feed/overview).

Our inference: book updates should improve observation opportunities for
thin products whose prices or sizes change between executions. This is a
testable source-quality hypothesis; the documentation does not promise a
fresh quote on every 5s cutoff or measure our consumer's performance.

The next book adapter must apply a whole batch atomically after a validated
snapshot, replace sizes rather than add them, delete zero levels, and expose
the best valid bid/ask and associated sizes. A reconnect, lost subscription,
invalid/crossed book, queue loss or processing failure requires an explicit
unavailable state and a new validated snapshot. Do not splice an old book
across connections or mix Exchange and Advanced Trade message schemas.

For the first source diagnostic, preserve the existing **10s maximum age from
the last actual book snapshot/update receipt**. A heartbeat must not refresh
the quote timestamp. Record book-change age, observation age, connection,
subscription state and processing/availability time separately. An unchanged
but continuously verified book could justify a different future policy only
through a versioned contract amendment, never a silently refreshed timestamp.
Batch observations are not a full intrabatch price path or continuous F3
availability. Keep missing sizes/quotes unavailable and never use last trade
price as a hidden fallback. Quiet books may still fail the initial TTL.

## Resolve publication timing before collecting study rows

There is a structural latency boundary: a heartbeat received after window end
can certify that earlier window only after receipt and processing. Emitting
health every 5 seconds cannot eliminate it. For example, a window ending at
10,000ms with its proving heartbeat at 10,050ms and processing complete at
10,060ms is unavailable for a 10,000ms decision, even if counts later match.

Best-practice direction for the next draft: use the latest certified feature
window available at the **actual publication time**, retain its window end
and age, enforce a declared maximum lag, and measure the forward outcome
from publication. This requires an explicit timing/target amendment before
use: current `Sample` and target contracts tie input and outcome anchors to
the same 5s cutoff. Do not put late verification into an earlier `Sample`,
shift a label backwards, drop the final unverified tail as if it were zero,
or silently change the target clock. Measure source/processing/proof lag in
the next data-only pilot before choosing a numerical lag limit. No limit
has been fitted or declared empirically validated here.

## Next bounded work

Implement and test the offline book adapter and explicit publication-timing
draft next. Then predeclare a bounded source/latency pilot and instrument
actual receipt, processing and durable-publication timestamps. This checkpoint
does not launch that pilot or modify the always-on collector. Allow at least
62 **complete** warmup minutes before eligible decisions, retain final outcome
maturity separately, and count at least 14 valid shadow days only after the
source/readiness and study rules are frozen. Raw elapsed time cannot replace
valid coverage. Feature formulas, stages, model/readiness and meaningful-effect
rules still require implementation/freeze. No fitting or live ranking change
follows from passing engineering checks.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 43 | docs/research/INSTANT_BOOK_AND_PUBLICATION_20261103.md | sha256=9ccbc147339d9aa66755a4b5d428eac354ac94ba15abedfd1e506df1409fb1e0 =====
# Offline book state and publication-clock draft

November 3, 2026 (Pacific). This implements the next bounded step from the
[October 30 policy](INSTANT_COVERAGE_AND_QUOTE_POLICY_20261030.md).
The existing target evaluator, V1/V2 target JSON, recorder, baseline builder
and locked historical comparison remain unchanged. New code is offline only.

## Book adapter

`scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_book.py` implements `BookReplay` under
[a separate versioned contract](INSTANT_BOOK_CONTRACT_V1_DRAFT.json). It
consumes the existing receipt envelope, using the monitor for bounded packet,
clock, subscription and connection checks. It performs no network access.

The official Exchange channel specification defines a complete initial
snapshot, then absolute price-level sizes, with zero deleting a level.
`level2_batch` uses that schema in 50ms batches, with its exchange time referring
to the latest event in the batch.
[Coinbase channel reference](https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/exchange/websocket-feed/channels).
Our implementation uses receipt order and preserves exchange time as metadata.
It does not assume contiguous exchange sequence numbers for selected channels.

- Acknowledged `level2_batch`, `matches` and `heartbeat` memberships are required
  for each declared product. A valid book starts from a fresh snapshot.
- Decimal prices/sizes retain their exact level identity. Amounts must be
  finite, nonnegative and representable for downstream floating readers;
  prices and resting snapshot sizes must be positive. Duplicate snapshot
  prices, empty sides, and final locked/crossed books are unavailable.
- Update batches apply atomically. Absolute sizes replace prior quantities;
  zero deletes; repeated changes at a price use the final change. Temporary
  intrabatch crossings do not create synthetic intermediate quotes or peaks.
- An invalid batch discards book continuity until a new valid snapshot.
  Connection/subscription loss, queue/transport/audit failures require a fresh
  acknowledgement and snapshot. A heartbeat gap above 5s requires a snapshot.
  Stale heartbeat and stale quote are separately reported.
- Last actual book observation, whole-book change and BBO change times remain
  distinct. A real deep-level or no-op update is an observation; only an actual
  level/BBO change moves the corresponding change clock. Heartbeats and ticker
  packets never refresh the book observation. The original 10s observation TTL
  remains in force, with exactly 10s allowed.
- Processing completion is measured after book construction. Read-only frozen
  results retain that availability time and source/contract/monitor hashes.
  Queries filter receipt strictly before cutoff and knowledge at or before
  the requested as-of. Explicit later diagnostics do not rewrite earlier as-of
  views. A later failure becomes visible only when known.

`view(product, cutoff, asof=...)` describes the last **fully processed** state
then known. An in-flight/queued update is not certified by an older visible
quote. `full_feed_verified` and `target_sample_adapter_supported` stay false.
This is not a queue-durability certificate, a continuous F3 integral, a full
intrabatch path, or a bridge that silently substitutes books into legacy
`ReplayStore`/`Sample`. Historical replay uses today's processing clock;
the injected clock in synthetic tests is not authenticated historical evidence.

At most 100,000 levels per product are allowed. Packet/product limits inherit
the monitor's 100,000 packets/32 products. This is a bounded offline prototype,
not a claim that a multi-product one-hour book capture fits those limits.
Version and test streaming persistence/rotation, real connection identities,
queue-loss reporting and processing/durable-publication evidence before a
source pilot. Do not raise a cap merely to make an old sample pass.

## Publication timing

`scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_publication.py` creates immutable clock
diagnostics under the [publication contract](INSTANT_PUBLICATION_CONTRACT_V1_DRAFT.json).
It requires:

```text
feature_cutoff <= inputs_available <= decision_completed <= published
feature_cutoff lies on the existing 5s grid
```

The first inequality concerns all completed-window inputs and coverage, not
the timestamp of one old quote. Publication ID and product identity are
explicit. A window ending at 10,000ms, verified at 10,060ms, computed at
10,080ms and published at 10,100ms has 60ms verification lag, 100ms feature age
and 20ms decision-to-publication delay. It cannot be an input known at 10,000ms.

Legacy V1/V2 targets retain the decision cutoff as their anchor. A proposed
future publication-anchored target must use actual publication for its entry
reference and forward horizon, with an explicit policy for off-grid entry
prices and label maturity. Do not round entry backwards, silently shorten a
horizon, or relabel a cutoff outcome as publication performance. The new
module flags that an amendment is required; it implements no new outcome.

No numerical feature-age limit has been selected. A supplied diagnostic limit
can show an exact age comparison, but never enables fitting. That limit and
off-grid entry/price-availability rules must be predeclared and frozen after
the data-only source/latency diagnostic, before outcome inspection. Caller
timestamps remain trusted assertions; the function cannot prove a durable
publication or independently validate source completeness.

Keep immutable decision inputs separate from later grading knowledge.
Subsequent negative feed evidence must remain available to a future grading
policy; it must not rewrite what the decision knew. The current legacy sample
does not carry that later evidence, so this remains an explicit amendment
requirement rather than a silent target change.

## Checks, ownership and next work

54 new synthetic cases cover hand-calculated BBO/pressure, absolute quantities,
atomic multi-change batches, malformed amounts/books, stale observations,
clock boundaries/completion, reconnect and subscription/audit failures,
unavailable states, immutable history, and publication chronology/age bounds.
No real `level2_batch` data or empirical improvement is claimed.

The existing Gemini/Claude target and offline reviews remain accepted. Claude's
new monitor/sample review at `91f3043` is now independently reproduced and
accepted as `652f5af`; read the
[acceptance and remaining amendments](INSTANT_COVERAGE_REVIEW_ACCEPTANCE_20261103.md).
The combined targeted suite passes 468 tests, including 54 new book/timing
cases and nine independent review probes. Reconciliation and later-grading
amendments still require explicit contracts. The held original `d711832`
remains unmerged; opt-in V2 already contains its adapted policy.

The [cloud association/exposure assignment](../ai/CLAUDE_CLOUD_ASSIGNMENT_20261103.md)
is dispatched by the user; its screenshot shows Fable 5.1 reading the required
sources. Implementation/push is pending. It owns new association/exposure
code on its own branch. Codex owns book/timing here. Do not mark the study frozen until its
executable rules, review and remaining source/model/readiness gates are done.

Next: declare/verify bounded reordering reconciliation and later-grading
evidence, then instrument/validate the
streaming source and publication path before a predeclared pilot. Preserve
the four-product gate, >=62 complete warmup minutes, final label maturity,
14 valid shadow days, no physical-onset inference and locked historical
validation. Fitting, new captures and production integration stay off.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 44 | docs/research/INSTANT_INPUT_EVIDENCE_20261104.md | sha256=b3e8ca3b06c7ba448e30a6564f86beaa14dac7f4395aad4458d735f2a64e9eaf =====
# Planned inventory and timed input evidence, stage 1

November 4, 2026 (Pacific). Implementation **`8607a36`** adds a bounded,
offline foundation for the eligibility producer. **1,195 targeted tests pass**,
including 32 new cases, on Windows Python 3.12.10 / pytest 8.3.5. Black 24.8.0,
guardrails and all 11 historical hashes pass. Subsequent independent review `f1ab7d5` is accepted as `086eb20`;
1,241 targeted tests pass with no code patch. Read
[the November 6 acceptance](INSTANT_INPUT_REVIEW_ACCEPTANCE_20261106.md).

This stage completes planned-slot accounting and measured packet/trade
processing records. It does **not** complete positive study eligibility:
baseline/feature evidence, full input-health requirements and publication
policy are still missing. Every planned row explicitly reports unavailable
eligibility. It supplies no boolean bridge into the directional reporter.

Code: `scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_input_evidence.py`.
[Contract](INSTANT_INPUT_EVIDENCE_CONTRACT_V1_DRAFT.json),
[checks](results/instant_input_evidence_20261104/CHECKS.json) and
[review prompt](../ai/CLAUDE_INPUT_EVIDENCE_REVIEW_20261104.md).

## Inventory accounting

The intent is the static cross product of declared products, every 5s cutoff
in `[first_cutoff, stop_cutoff)` and both directions. BTC is required as the
benchmark. Every expected row is retained, including products with no packet,
missing quotes or no proven trade interval. Empty observations do not shrink
the declared inventory or become healthy zero counts.

The canonical intent and its hash are separate from the time the recorder
actually loaded it. A file hash does not prove it was declared in advance.
`inventory_precedes_first_cutoff` requires the measured loading time to be
strictly earlier. Loading an old plan during replay records today's time and
does not claim prospective declaration. This validates completeness relative
to the submitted plan; it cannot independently prove that the plan contains
the intended exchange universe. That universe still needs an external,
predeclared identity/source review.

Limits are 10,000 inventory rows, 32 products, the existing 100,000-packet
monitor bound and existing book-level bounds. These are offline guards, not
a measured one-hour capacity profile or new capture permission. A tape must
start at local receipt sequence 1 and remain contiguous. Bounds, malformed
packets or clock regression fail the artifact closed. Sealing requires all
planned cutoff times to have been reached; no prefix is certified a complete capture.
An empty tape can yield complete **inventory accounting** with all rows
unavailable; it is never called a complete or healthy capture.

## Processing and component evidence

Packet evidence stores receipt sequence/time, measured processing start and
completion, availability at least receipt plus 1ms, and canonical envelope
hash. Unique live-match rows retain their first receipt, packet completion,
connection and continuity epoch, exact Decimal price/size and inverted maker
side. Unknown sides remain separate. REST recovery, ticker sizes and
`last_match` do not create execution rows; duplicates never move first receipt.
Completion measures book/trade payload processing, not disk commit or durable
publication. Injected clocks remain trusted synthetic-test inputs.

Coin and BTC evidence are computed separately:

- The quote component uses the existing last processed book view and original
  observation age. Its selected state and heartbeat must also have completed
  this producer's processing by the requested knowledge time. A heartbeat
  cannot refresh quote age; an unfinished quote cannot borrow book-only
  completion. No continuous 30s F3 path is certified.
- The 30s trade component uses exact receipt-ID proofs plus completed trade
  rows, scoped to the verifying connection and continuity epoch. A first
  receipt at a shared-millisecond reset stays in its original epoch and cannot
  be certified by the following epoch. Known raw counts remain diagnostic;
  verified counts/amounts stay null without these checks. Only a proven empty
  receipt window becomes zero. Unknown taker side prevents signed-flow use.

`checkpoint(cutoff)` freezes the full product/direction cohort with component
knowledge at that cutoff and a separately measured assessment-completion
time. Returned rows are frozen and their nested component payload is serialized;
decoded copies cannot mutate them. Later packets, conflicts or failures never
rewrite the frozen cohort. Local logger/clock corruption still fails the whole
artifact closed, rather than exporting earlier rows from a failed run.

`component_evidence(product, cutoff, asof=later)` is a separately marked later
diagnostic. For a window ending at 30,000ms, its closing heartbeat at 30,000ms
is not available before 30,001ms even with no processing delay. The later
diagnostic can then report a proven zero; the frozen 30,000ms row remains
unproven. Neither result is full-feed or quote-continuity certification.

## API and offline artifacts

```python
from instant_input_evidence import InputEvidenceRecorder

recorder = InputEvidenceRecorder(products, first_cutoff, stop_cutoff)
for saved_envelope in saved_packets:
    recorder.ingest(saved_envelope)
report = recorder.seal()
```

The CLI takes a saved JSONL/gzip tape and a JSON inventory containing
`products`, `first_cutoff` and `stop_cutoff`:

```sh
python scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_input_evidence.py packets.jsonl.gz --inventory inventory.json --out-dir ../new-evidence-output
```

Output must be new and outside both input directories. It contains
`packet_processing.jsonl`, `trade_processing.jsonl` and `REPORT.json` with
inventory, complete planned rows, source/ledger hashes and closed gates.
Input hashes are checked before/after. Failures retain `FAILURE.json`; a
failed/partial output is not an eligibility artifact. Output files are written
at finalization, so the ledger does not establish durable availability at the
earlier processing times. No socket, account, production or database is opened.

## Review and remaining work

The 32 new cases include synthetic CLI round trip, planned omissions,
payload completion versus book completion, late proofs, epoch-boundary
certification, immutable cohorts, exact signed amounts, unknown sides,
deduplication/conflicts, stale and missing benchmark quotes, caps and clocks.
The epoch-boundary counterexample failed during implementation before the new
layer's owner/epoch check and passes afterward. Existing adapters/contracts,
targets/V1/V2, historical results, backend and frontend are unchanged. No real
capture/replay, market outcome experiment, fit, native Mac or full-suite run
is claimed. All new evidence needed for review is generated by committed tests.

The stage-1 review is complete. Next: build actual timed baseline and
F1-F5 evidence and declare publication/entry/age policy. Do not infer warmup
from elapsed time, turn scoped ID checks into full health, or fabricate ready
features. Later grading, real upstream connection identity, streaming limits
and durable publication remain open. Preserve the original feed gate, >=62
complete warmup minutes, locked historical validation and V1 default. The
study is unfrozen and fitting/live scoring remain unchanged.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 45 | docs/research/INSTANT_TIMED_BASELINE_20261106.md | sha256=5f38f7da2385eb98c38fc03b8a85350725235a1e151a2da116ae0e66f3ebad89 =====
# Timed volatility and activity baseline draft

The offline `build_timed_baseline(recorder, product, cutoff, asof=...)` API
takes a declared product and 5-second feature cutoff. It computes two
independent components from the timed input-evidence ledger.

- **Volatility and BTC beta.** Use 61 fresh, paired minute-close midpoints
  to form 60 log returns. The final close is
  `floor((cutoff_ms - 35000) / 60000) * 60000`. Use the coin's sample
  standard deviation and centered OLS against BTC. A flat BTC return series
  gives beta 1 with an explicit fallback flag. The trade receipt proof covers
  the preceding 5-second bucket at the first close through the final close.
- **Activity pace.** Use 120 half-open 30-second receipt windows ending at
  `cutoff_ms - 30000`. Median trade count and quote USD amount use first
  receipts. One proof chain within a single continuity epoch covers the
  full hour. A proved empty bin contributes zero to both medians. When a
  proof is missing, the component is unavailable. Maker side is irrelevant
  for these unsigned medians.

A missing benchmark close can block volatility while pace is ready. Each
ready component records the latest availability of the quote or trade
evidence it used, including the producer's payload completion. The
evidence as-of can precede the feature cutoff once a component's window
has closed. The plan must already be recorded by that as-of, and the as-of
must have elapsed on the recorder clock. The builder reads the clock again
after calculation and provenance hashing to record computation completion.
A later calculation with the same evidence as-of can use the same evidence
while having a later completion timestamp.

The result pins the inventory hash and its recorded time, the packet count
and SHA-256 of the known canonical packet prefix, and normalized source
and contract hashes. The draft is for offline component calculation.
Positive eligibility still requires feature, quote-health and publication
evidence. Throughput and availability on a real capture remain to be
measured.

The five new synthetic cases cover delayed proof completion, stable
prefix provenance, BTC quote missingness, verified empty versus unknown
activity, a trade exactly at the exclusive right boundary, and the
inventory and processing clocks. See the
[check record](results/instant_timed_baseline_20261106/CHECKS.json).

Next, define F3's book observation integration and F4's candle source and
alignment, then build cutoff-bound F1–F5 feature evidence. The data-only
pilot will measure coverage and latency after the remaining interfaces and
pilot rules are in place.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 46 | docs/research/INSTANT_TIMED_FEATURES_20261107.md | sha256=198db0490c6d6040ac9c3bd1d6bb4ab55be7e2957d4ecb90a4bd3aae77cf1b76 =====
# Timed instant-radar feature draft

The offline builder computes the five candidate inputs for one declared
product, 5-second feature cutoff `b`, and direction. The sign is +1 for up
and −1 for down. Each component has its own value or missingness reason.
The formulas use the candidate's fixed 30s and 180s windows. Sigma uses
the declared 0.0015 one-minute floor. The accompanying diagnostics retain
velocity, unsigned activity, cutoff spread, expansion, and raw signed
180s return for comparisons.

| Input | Computation |
| --- | --- |
| F1 acceleration | Direction times (30s midpoint log return minus one fifth of the preceding 150s log return) divided by floored sigma. Endpoints are `b-180s`, `b-30s`, and `b`. |
| F2 signed participation | Signed taker USD imbalance times log1p of 30s USD pace relative to the timed 120-bin median. Use at least five distinct first-receipt matches in `[b-30s,b)`, known maker sides inverted to taker sides, positive USD, and a positive baseline median. |
| F3 sampled pressure | Signed time-weighted mean of `(bid_size-ask_size)/(bid_size+ask_size)` over `[b-30s,b)`. |
| F4 active compression and expansion | Directional positive 30s velocity divided by `1 + log(max(high)/min(low))/(sigma*sqrt(15))`. |
| F5 market-relative movement | Signed `(coin_180s - beta * BTC_180s) / sigma` from synchronized midpoint endpoints. Retain the beta fallback flag. |

## F3 boundaries

Use fully processed book and transport events known by the evidence as-of,
ordered by local receipt and then sequence. Carry the last known state into
the window. At a receipt timestamp the final state in that millisecond applies
to the following segment. Events at `b` are excluded. Integrate only across
segments with a valid BBO, a matching active heartbeat, a quote no older than
10s, and a heartbeat no older than 5s. Age is measured from the original
receipt. Reaching an age limit at a segment's right edge is allowed. Any
positive-duration gap makes F3 unavailable. The denominator is 30 seconds.
The result measures sampled book pressure at logger receipts.

## F4 boundaries

Build high and low from actual first-receipt matches in 15 half-open,
UTC-aligned one-minute bins. The last bin ends at
`floor((b-30s)/60000)*60000` so its trades precede the expansion window.
One receipt-ID proof chain within a single continuity epoch must cover the
15-minute window. Count certified empty minutes separately and leave their
high and low absent. Require at least ten traded minutes. An incomplete
proof makes F4 unavailable. These are receipt-time trade candles.

## Timing and scope

A result carries the observation cutoff, explicit evidence as-of, each
ready component's evidence-availability time, actual baseline and feature
computation completion, and the timed baseline's evidence-prefix provenance.
Post-cutoff receipts cannot enter the formulas. A proof that becomes known
later can verify pre-cutoff observations at an explicitly later as-of. The
result identifies computation after the cutoff as diagnostic. An input
evidence timestamp does not include the subsequent baseline or feature
calculation. A consumer must use the recorded computation completion times.
This API produces offline evidence for the separately versioned decision
and grading design. A data-only pilot still needs to measure real
coverage and latency before a decision age limit or study freeze.

The detailed candidate and its comparison plan are recorded in the
[study draft](INSTANT_STUDY_FREEZE_CANDIDATE_20261030.md) and
[methodology triage](INSTANT_METHOD_REVIEW_TRIAGE_20261106.md).

===== END SOURCE 46 =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE 47 | docs/research/INSTANT_DELAYED_DECISION_20261107.md | sha256=69ac0074fbf5ecb98e15c53eeb184a75146ec2fe46252f5ed9633b31087a9ff5 =====
# Delayed decision evidence and later cutoff grading

The offline module `instant_delayed_decision.py` adds two draft record versions,
`instant-delayed-decision-v1-draft` and `instant-later-cutoff-grade-v1-draft`.

## Decision and publication clocks

`prepare_decision` builds a timed F1–F5 row for a declared slot. It freezes
canonical feature and paired anchor-quote bytes and measures preparation
completion after serialization. The snapshot retains the observation cutoff
`b`, evidence as-of, feature completion, and decision preparation completion.
All observations used in the feature values precede `b`. A later evidence
as-of can admit later proof of a pre-cutoff interval. Unavailable features
stay unavailable in that snapshot.

`observe_publication` associates the snapshot with a caller-supplied
publication identity and timestamp. It validates this chronology and
measures when the observation was recorded:

```text
b <= decision evidence as-of <= feature computation completed
  <= snapshot preparation completed <= observed publication <= observation recorded
```

The observation uses the feature computation completion as the time all
computed inputs could be available, while the feature row preserves the
individual evidence clocks. The supplied timestamp is an observation
assertion. Durable delivery and scoring are separate work. An optional
diagnostic age bound uses the existing publication helper. Study selection
of an age limit awaits measured source and processing latency.

## Later grading

`grade_publication` returns an independent diagnostic view at a later
as-of. It checks the decision's inventory, ledger prefix and source provenance,
and records the grading prefix and decision digest. The requested as-of
must have elapsed and include the publication observation. It returns
`pending` through the period before `b+H`, with `H` equal to 300 or 900
seconds. Once mature, absent or contradicted evidence returns
`unavailable` with a null outcome. A mature grade with adequate evidence
returns a Boolean `cutoff_verified_forward`.

For this draft the published snapshot needs all five features available.
Grading rechecks the frozen inputs' trade proof windows and both products'
receipt-ID coverage from the 5s bucket preceding the anchor through
the horizon. Later conflicts in these windows can invalidate a grade.
As-ofs that fall during a packet's processing are unavailable once the
horizon is mature. This conservative boundary keeps a monitor result
from reaching grading before the recorder finishes the packet. The
original snapshot and earlier evidence views remain independently
reproducible.

The outcome uses paired, fresh receipt-time midpoints at each 5s endpoint,
the frozen anchor and volatility/beta, and first-receipt matches strictly
inside `(b,b+H)`. The terminal residual, spread and sigma threshold, peak
retention and forward match floor follow the pinned V2 impulse rules.
A contradiction in the anchor quote vetoes grading. A different outcome
as-of can change the grade's evidence without substituting new decision
inputs. Publication time is reported separately from the fixed cutoff
horizon. The grade includes whether publication preceded the horizon end.

This draft measures a fixed-cutoff outcome on scoped evidence. A later
publication-anchored quote benchmark needs its own entry and maturity
rules. The recorder and publication path still need durable clock and
connection evidence before a data-only coverage and latency pilot.

See the [check record](results/instant_delayed_decision_20261107/CHECKS.json).

===== END SOURCE 47 =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE 48 | docs/research/INSTANT_STREAM_JOURNAL_20261107.md | sha256=62eaa31855d3604c021b7d4cacbb1698e7240f91cfc694e14dd8afa80d89f3c6 =====
# Streaming input journal and local publication receipts

`StreamJournal` is the bounded offline-testable recording boundary for
the instant-radar pilot. It uses one writer thread and one active
transport per segment. An adapter calls `open_connection` once for each
new socket, `receive` for each frame, and `end_connection` at shutdown or
loss. Each connection gets a unique segment-and-open ID. A closed or
failed connection cannot submit later frames under its old ID.

The journal stamps wall and monotonic receipt clocks at the callback
boundary. It processes the canonical WebSocket envelope through the
timed input ledger, then commits the envelope, packet processing clocks
and first-receipt trade evidence together. The caller receives the
separately measured time of the commit return. Frame decode failures,
transport errors and reported queue losses create explicit barriers.
Malformed frames retain a hash and byte count. A write error stops the
writer. The last committed prefix remains independently readable.

## Storage and restart

Use a fresh local SQLite file per segment. The writer requires rollback
journaling in DELETE mode with EXTRA synchronization. Creation is exclusive.
The bounds are at most 100,000 packets, 1 MiB per incoming frame, and
256 MiB of logical record payload per segment. Lifecycle records also
count toward the packet cap. A smaller packet or record limit can be
supplied. The payload limit excludes SQLite page and journal overhead.
The recorder retains the existing bounded in-memory evidence state.

`finish` marks an explicit end after the final connection closes.
`read_segment`, `iter_packets` and `read_local_publication` recover committed
records. The readers verify record digests and packet sequence. A file
still marked recording is an unfinished prefix. Open a fresh segment after
a stop or restart. Each new segment has independent connection IDs and
warmup evidence. There is no proof chain across segment boundaries.
Recovered processing fields are observations from the original writer.
Any new replay of the envelopes measures its own processing.

## Local publication receipts

`append_publication` verifies the frozen decision's inventory, recorder
source and packet prefix. It commits the decision and publication ID
before reading the local commit clock, then persists that observation
in a second transaction. Repeating the same ID and decision returns
the original receipt. Reusing the ID for a different decision fails.
If the process exits between commits, the entry is recoverable with
an absent commit observation. It remains unacknowledged.

The receipt describes local database persistence. External delivery needs
a separate observed publication time. The draft accepts incomplete
diagnostic snapshots to preserve their missingness. Local receipt,
publication delivery and grading remain distinct.

## Pilot preparation

Socket ownership and callback wiring come next. The synchronous recording
path has no application queue. The pilot must measure callback lag,
transport backlog, processing, storage latency and capacity on the
intended host. The bounds are engineering limits and the observed
callback time may follow arrival at the socket. The prototype uses
synthetic input and a real subprocess exit for crash recovery. Host
power-loss behavior and end-to-end feed coverage require separate evidence.

See the [check record](results/instant_stream_journal_20261107/CHECKS.json).

===== END SOURCE 48 =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE 49 | docs/research/INSTANT_ASSOCIATION_EXPOSURE_20261103.md | sha256=675c740146c8a76cb5ef150552b08ce72357ff873c7ad98651f092c31efac8ca =====
# Offline association and exposure reporting draft — 2026-11-03

Implemented by a Claude Code cloud session on branch
`claude/instant-association-cloud-20261103`, based on Codex tip `1d394e4`
(book/publication `8c51878`, integrated monitor review `652f5af`). This is the
[cloud assignment](../ai/CLAUDE_CLOUD_ASSIGNMENT_20261103.md). **Pending Codex
review; not merged.** It is a standalone offline reporting module with a
versioned draft contract. It changes no target label, recorder, monitor,
sample, book or publication code, no existing contract, no frozen artifact
and no live service. Fitting, operational rates and physical onset stay off.

Code: `scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_association.py`. Contract:
[`INSTANT_ASSOCIATION_CONTRACT_V1_DRAFT.json`](INSTANT_ASSOCIATION_CONTRACT_V1_DRAFT.json).
Tests: `tests/test_instant_association.py`. Record:
[`results/instant_association_20261103/CHECKS.json`](results/instant_association_20261103/CHECKS.json).

## Primary pointwise outcomes stay fixed

`association_report(labels, alerts, asof_ts_ms=...)` takes complete v2 labels
(`instant-target-v2-draft`, one contract hash and one evaluator hash) and alert
records with `publication_id`, `product_id`, `direction`, `anchor_ts_ms`,
`inputs_available_ts_ms` and `published_ts_ms`. For each alert and **each
horizon separately** it looks up `verified_forward` at the alert's declared
decision-cutoff anchor:

| Label at (product, direction, horizon, anchor) | Primary outcome |
|---|---|
| `ready`, `verified_forward` True | `success` |
| `ready`, `verified_forward` False | `failure` |
| `pending`, `unavailable` or absent | `ungradable`, reason retained, outside both denominators |

`success_rate_among_graded` divides by success plus failure only;
`graded_fraction` shows how much of the alert set that covers. A ready label
whose outcome is not a boolean, an incomplete label carrying an outcome, a
ready label maturing after the as-of, a v1 label, mixed provenance, duplicate
identities, off-grid anchors and malformed rows are rejected. Nothing missing
is coerced to false.

Alerts carry no horizon: every alert is graded in both tables because the
study evaluates 300s and 900s outcomes separately. `publication_id` is the
retained identity and must be unique.

## Forward-window coverage diagnostic

Windows are the deduplicated verified forward windows from
`instant_diagnostics.forward_catalog_report`, so left-censor flags come from
the existing reporting layer unchanged. Per product, direction and horizon,
windows are sorted by anchor and each receives the earliest unused alert with
actual publication in `[anchor - 180s, forward_peak]`, ordering alerts by
`published_ts_ms` and then `publication_id`. One alert covers at most one
window per horizon; the same alert may cover a 5m window and a 15m window.
Unmatched windows and alerts are listed. Rates are named
`uncovered_forward_window_rate` and `conditional_catalog_coverage_rate`;
`physical_missed_move_rate` is always null and `association_kind` is
`forward_window_not_physical_impulse`.

The report counts `failed_alerts_that_cover_a_forward_window` and states
`association_alters_primary_outcomes: false`. Three evaluator-graded tests
make this executable: a 5m alert whose move begins at +340s and a 15m alert
whose move begins at +940s both cover the later catalog window and both remain
pointwise failures; an alert published at a completed window's endpoint,
where the forward peak equals maturity, covers that window and is still a
failure at its own anchor.

**Known property for review.** Earliest-unused assignment is the declared
rule from the freeze candidate, implemented literally. It is not a maximum
matching: when a window's lead edge overlaps the previous window's tail, the
greedy choice can leave a later window uncovered although a different pairing
would cover both. The rule is transparent and deterministic; changing it is a
contract amendment, not a tuning step.

## Exposure from explicit eligibility records

`exposure_report(labels, eligibility, asof_ts_ms=..., ranker_slots=None)` takes
one record per product and 5s cutoff with four explicit booleans:
`universe_member`, `warmup_complete`, `health_verified`, `candidate_eligible`.
A slot is **decision-eligible** when all four hold. Exclusions are counted by
the first failing flag in that order. Duplicate or overlapping slots, missing
or non-boolean flags, off-grid cutoffs and cutoffs after the as-of are rejected.

- `decision_eligible_product_hours` sums eligible slots across products.
  `board_wall_clock_hours_with_any_eligible_product` counts the union of
  cutoffs with at least one eligible product; it is never the sum. A separate
  union counts cutoffs with any verified health.
- `universe_size_by_cutoff` lists the universe size at every cutoff, with
  first, last, minimum and maximum, so a shrinking universe stays visible.
- Per horizon, an eligible slot is **retrospectively gradable** only if its
  trailing horizon matured by the as-of and both direction labels are
  `ready`. Immature, missing and incomplete cases are counted by reason.
  The decision-eligible counts never depend on labels, so missing future
  data cannot erase the decision universe.
- `comparison` distinguishes identical complete rows (eligible, gradable and
  scored by every supplied ranker) from each ranker's natural coverage of
  eligible and of gradable slots. Ranker slots must be recorded slots.

Eligibility flags are caller assertions. This module does not verify
health, warmup or universe membership itself; the monitor, book adapter and
future recorder supply that evidence under their own contracts.

## Clocks and timing

Each alert retains its input anchor, input availability and actual
publication separately, with derived `input_lag_ms`, `publication_delay_ms`
and `inputs_known_at_anchor`. Records must satisfy anchor <= inputs available
<= published <= as-of; a violation is rejected, so no late feature is accepted
as known at an earlier anchor. The primary outcome is graded at the declared
decision-cutoff anchor, as the current V1/V2 contracts require. A
publication-anchored target remains a separate amendment; this module does
not shift any label.

# Direction-specific exposure reporting draft

November 4, 2026 (Pacific). This opt-in reporting addition follows the
accepted cloud association work and corrected reconciliation checkpoint
`3c859ef`. It resolves the report's directional-denominator choice; the
evidence-producing eligibility adapter and overall study remain unfinished.
Claude's independent review `583a73a` is accepted as **`4925ac5`** with no
defect found and no code patch. **1,163 targeted tests pass**, including 51
new independent probes. Read
[the acceptance](INSTANT_DIRECTIONAL_REVIEW_ACCEPTANCE_20261104.md).

Implementation **`9da4987`** passes **1,112 targeted tests**, including 48
new directional cases. Black 24.8.0, guardrails and all 11 historical hashes
pass on Windows Python 3.12.10 / pytest 8.3.5. Existing implementation and
contracts are unchanged. No full-suite, native Mac or independent-review pass
was claimed at that initial checkpoint. The acceptance above records the
subsequent independent review and Windows verification.

Code: `scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_directional_exposure.py`.
[Contract](INSTANT_DIRECTIONAL_EXPOSURE_CONTRACT_V1_DRAFT.json),
[checks](results/instant_directional_exposure_20261104/CHECKS.json) and
[independent review assignment](../ai/CLAUDE_DIRECTIONAL_EXPOSURE_REVIEW_20261104.md).

## Per-board denominators

The up and down boards get separate decision-eligible and retrospectively
gradable rows at 300s and 900s. A row is gradable when its own direction's
label is ready and mature by report as-of. A ready negative outcome is still
gradable. Missing, pending and unavailable outcomes retain their reasons;
they never become failures. A missing opposite-direction label does not
remove the valid board's row.

The original `instant_association.exposure_report` remains an unchanged,
separately named joint both-direction diagnostic. Greedy event association,
pointwise targets, publication clocks and all existing contracts are unchanged.
This addition defines no physical onset or operational missed-move rate.

## Explicit eligibility input

```python
from instant_directional_exposure import exposure_report

out = exposure_report(
    v2_labels,
    directional_eligibility,
    asof_ts_ms=report_time,
    ranker_slots={"baseline": [("TEST-USD", "up", cutoff)]},
)
```

Each eligibility record contains `product_id`, `direction`, 5s-grid `ts_ms`,
`available_ts_ms` and the four actual booleans `universe_member`,
`warmup_complete`, `health_verified`, `candidate_eligible`. All four must be
true for decision eligibility. The assertion must be known by its cutoff,
which must not exceed report as-of. A delayed proof cannot be relabelled as
known at an earlier cutoff; a future publication-time adapter needs its own
declared semantics.

Both directions must explicitly account for each submitted product/cutoff,
with the same universe membership. Other flags may differ by board. Unpaired
records are rejected; universe counts use up-board membership only because
paired equal membership is enforced. Relaxing either rule requires revisiting
the counting rule. Malformed records, duplicates and coerced booleans are
rejected. These checks cannot
detect products or cutoffs omitted from both boards: a complete intended
inventory and independently verified evidence are still producer requirements.
The reporter explicitly identifies eligibility as **caller assertions**, not
authenticated monitor/book evidence. It does not accept monitor proofs as
full-feed or quote certification and creates no live eligibility records.

Future labels never change decision exposure. Exclusions use the existing
flag precedence, and reason counts are exclusive by first failed flag.
Product-hours count eligible product/cutoff slots. Board hours count the
union of eligible cutoffs per board; the either-board clock is a further
union, so concurrent products and boards do not multiply elapsed time.

Ranker slots carry `(product_id, direction, ts_ms)` triples and must refer to
recorded eligibility rows. Per board/horizon, identical comparison rows are
eligible, own-direction gradable and scored by every supplied ranker. Their
identities and counts are reported alongside natural per-ranker coverage.
With no ranker mapping, the complete-row table describes all gradable eligible
rows; it is not evidence that any model scored them.

## Boundaries and next work

Synthetic hand-calculated tests cover opposite-direction missingness, true
negative gradability, horizon maturity, ranker intersections, time unions,
causal cutoffs, exclusions and malformed inputs. The existing association
tests verify its joint and greedy behavior still holds. No market data or
historical outcome experiment is run for this addition.

The independent review is complete. Next is a separately versioned
eligibility/grading evidence producer with per-row completion, actual connection
identity, streaming bounds and durable publication. Publication entry/targets,
feature ages, F1-F5, stages/model/readiness and the full study still need freeze.
Preserve the original feed gate, >=62 complete warmup minutes, locked historical
validation and V1 default. Fitting and live integration remain off.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 61 | scripts/scoring_experiment/instant_association.py | sha256=0e79b8f1d609157d40f1ed3d34e7f01b85201c37489bc80e3b065d897bb485c3 =====
"""Offline association and exposure reporting. No labels, fits or live code change.

Each alert keeps its primary pointwise outcome at its declared decision-cutoff
anchor and fixed horizon. Forward-window coverage is a separately named
diagnostic; it never forgives a pointwise failure and defines no physical onset.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections import Counter
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path

import instant_diagnostics as diag


VERSION = "instant-association-exposure-v1-draft"
CONTRACT = (
    Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
    / "docs/research/INSTANT_ASSOCIATION_CONTRACT_V1_DRAFT.json"
)
TARGET_VERSION = "instant-target-v2-draft"
GRID_MS = 5000
HORIZONS = (300, 900)
LEAD_LIMIT_SECONDS = 180
DIRECTIONS = ("up", "down")
LABEL_KEYS = (
    "product_id",
    "anchor_ts_ms",
    "direction",
    "horizon_seconds",
    "status",
    "verified_forward",
    "label_maturity_ts_ms",
    "contract_version",
    "contract_sha256",
    "evaluator_sha256",
)
ALERT_KEYS = (
    "publication_id",
    "product_id",
    "direction",
    "anchor_ts_ms",
    "inputs_available_ts_ms",
    "published_ts_ms",
)
ELIGIBILITY_FLAGS = (
    "universe_member",
    "warmup_complete",
    "health_verified",
    "candidate_eligible",
)


def _integer(value):
    if type(value) is not int or value < 0:
        raise ValueError("nonnegative integer required")
    return value


def _grid(value):
    if _integer(value) % GRID_MS:
        raise ValueError("timestamps must lie on the 5s grid")
    return value


def _text(value):
    if not isinstance(value, str) or not value:
        raise ValueError("nonempty string identity required")
    return value


def _hours(slot_count):
    return slot_count * GRID_MS / 3_600_000


def provenance():
    raw = CONTRACT.read_text(encoding="utf-8").encode("utf-8")
    cfg = json.loads(raw)
    if (
        cfg["version"] != VERSION
        or cfg["target_version"] != TARGET_VERSION
        or cfg["grid_ms"] != GRID_MS
        or tuple(cfg["horizons_seconds"]) != HORIZONS
        or cfg["lead_limit_seconds"] != LEAD_LIMIT_SECONDS
        or cfg["fitting_allowed"] is not False
        or cfg["operational_missed_move_rates_enabled"] is not False
    ):
        raise ValueError("association contract and implementation differ")
    return dict(
        association_version=VERSION,
        association_contract_sha256=hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest(),
        association_implementation_sha256=hashlib.sha256(
            Path(__file__).read_text(encoding="utf-8").encode("utf-8")
        ).hexdigest(),
        evaluation_only=True,
        physical_onset_defined=False,
        operational_missed_move_rates_enabled=False,
        fitting_allowed=False,
    )


def _labels(labels, asof):
    """Index complete v2 labels by identity; reject mixed or malformed rows."""
    rows, contracts, evaluators = {}, set(), set()
    for row in labels:
        if not isinstance(row, dict) or any(k not in row for k in LABEL_KEYS):
            raise ValueError("malformed label row")
        if row["contract_version"] != TARGET_VERSION:
            raise ValueError("labels must come from the explicit v2 target draft")
        product, direction, horizon = (
            _text(row["product_id"]),
            row["direction"],
            row["horizon_seconds"],
        )
        if (
            direction not in DIRECTIONS
            or type(horizon) is not int
            or horizon not in HORIZONS
        ):
            raise ValueError("unsupported label direction or horizon")
        anchor, maturity = _grid(row["anchor_ts_ms"]), _integer(
            row["label_maturity_ts_ms"]
        )
        if maturity != anchor + horizon * 1000:
            raise ValueError("label maturity must equal anchor plus horizon")
        status, forward = row["status"], row["verified_forward"]
        if status == "ready":
            if type(forward) is not bool:
                raise ValueError("a ready label needs a boolean outcome; never coerce")
            if maturity > asof:
                raise ValueError("a ready label cannot mature after the report as-of")
            peak = row.get("peak_ts_ms")
            if type(peak) is not int or not anchor <= peak <= maturity:
                raise ValueError("a ready label needs a forward peak inside its window")
        elif status in ("pending", "unavailable"):
            if forward is not None:
                raise ValueError("an incomplete label cannot carry an outcome")
        else:
            raise ValueError("unknown label status")
        contracts.add(_text(row["contract_sha256"]))
        evaluators.add(_text(row["evaluator_sha256"]))
        key = (product, direction, horizon, anchor)
        if key in rows:
            raise ValueError("duplicate label identity")
        rows[key] = row
    if len(contracts) > 1 or len(evaluators) > 1:
        raise ValueError("cannot mix target contract or evaluator provenance")
    return rows, next(iter(contracts), None), next(iter(evaluators), None)


def _alerts(alerts, asof):
    """Retain anchor, input availability and actual publication separately."""
    out = {}
    for row in alerts:
        if not isinstance(row, dict) or any(k not in row for k in ALERT_KEYS):
            raise ValueError("malformed alert row")
        pid = _text(row["publication_id"])
        if pid in out:
            raise ValueError("duplicate publication identity")
        if row["direction"] not in DIRECTIONS:
            raise ValueError("unsupported alert direction")
        anchor, inputs, published = (
            _grid(row["anchor_ts_ms"]),
            _integer(row["inputs_available_ts_ms"]),
            _integer(row["published_ts_ms"]),
        )
        if not anchor <= inputs <= published <= asof:
            raise ValueError(
                "alert clocks must satisfy anchor <= inputs available <= published"
                " <= as-of; no late feature is known at an earlier anchor"
            )
        out[pid] = dict(
            publication_id=pid,
            product_id=_text(row["product_id"]),
            direction=row["direction"],
            anchor_ts_ms=anchor,
            inputs_available_ts_ms=inputs,
            published_ts_ms=published,
            input_lag_ms=inputs - anchor,
            publication_delay_ms=published - anchor,
            inputs_known_at_anchor=inputs == anchor,
        )
    return sorted(
        out.values(), key=lambda a: (a["published_ts_ms"], a["publication_id"])
    )


def _primary(label):
    if label is None:
        return "ungradable", "label_missing"
    if label["status"] == "ready":
        return ("success" if label["verified_forward"] else "failure"), None
    return "ungradable", f"{label['status']}:{label.get('reason') or 'no_reason'}"


def _ratio(numerator, denominator):
    return numerator / denominator if denominator else None


def association_report(labels, alerts, *, asof_ts_ms):
    """Primary pointwise outcomes per horizon plus forward-window coverage."""
    _integer(asof_ts_ms)
    rows, contract_sha256, evaluator_sha256 = _labels(labels, asof_ts_ms)
    pubs = _alerts(alerts, asof_ts_ms)
    windows = {}
    for window in diag.forward_catalog_report(list(rows.values())):
        key = window["product_id"], window["direction"], window["horizon_seconds"]
        windows.setdefault(key, []).append(window)
    lead_ms = LEAD_LIMIT_SECONDS * 1000
    tables, forgiven = {}, 0
    for horizon in HORIZONS:
        outcomes, counts, reasons = [], Counter(), Counter()
        for alert in pubs:
            label = rows.get(
                (
                    alert["product_id"],
                    alert["direction"],
                    horizon,
                    alert["anchor_ts_ms"],
                )
            )
            outcome, reason = _primary(label)
            counts[outcome] += 1
            if reason:
                reasons[reason] += 1
            outcomes.append(
                {
                    **alert,
                    "horizon_seconds": horizon,
                    "primary_outcome": outcome,
                    "ungradable_reason": reason,
                    "label_status": label["status"] if label else None,
                    "verified_forward": label["verified_forward"] if label else None,
                    "covers_forward_window_anchor_ts_ms": None,
                }
            )
        covered, uncovered, used = [], [], {}
        for (product, direction, h), group in sorted(windows.items()):
            if h != horizon:
                continue
            candidates = [
                a
                for a in pubs
                if a["product_id"] == product and a["direction"] == direction
            ]
            for window in sorted(group, key=lambda w: w["anchor_ts_ms"]):
                start, peak = window["anchor_ts_ms"] - lead_ms, window["peak_ts_ms"]
                chosen = next(
                    (
                        a
                        for a in candidates
                        if a["publication_id"] not in used
                        and start <= a["published_ts_ms"] <= peak
                    ),
                    None,
                )
                record = dict(
                    product_id=product,
                    direction=direction,
                    horizon_seconds=horizon,
                    anchor_ts_ms=window["anchor_ts_ms"],
                    forward_peak_ts_ms=peak,
                    label_maturity_ts_ms=window["label_maturity_ts_ms"],
                    edge_start_ts_ms=start,
                    left_censored=window["left_censored"],
                    left_censor_reason=window["left_censor_reason"],
                    covering_publication_id=None,
                    covering_published_ts_ms=None,
                    covering_offset_from_anchor_seconds=None,
                )
                if chosen is None:
                    uncovered.append(record)
                    continue
                used[chosen["publication_id"]] = window["anchor_ts_ms"]
                record.update(
                    covering_publication_id=chosen["publication_id"],
                    covering_published_ts_ms=chosen["published_ts_ms"],
                    covering_offset_from_anchor_seconds=(
                        chosen["published_ts_ms"] - window["anchor_ts_ms"]
                    )
                    / 1000,
                )
                covered.append(record)
        for row in outcomes:
            row["covers_forward_window_anchor_ts_ms"] = used.get(row["publication_id"])
            if row["primary_outcome"] == "failure" and row["publication_id"] in used:
                forgiven += 1
        unmatched = [
            a["publication_id"] for a in pubs if a["publication_id"] not in used
        ]
        window_count = len(covered) + len(uncovered)
        success, failure = counts["success"], counts["failure"]
        tables[str(horizon)] = dict(
            horizon_seconds=horizon,
            primary_pointwise=dict(
                alert_count=len(pubs),
                success_count=success,
                failure_count=failure,
                ungradable_count=counts["ungradable"],
                ungradable_reasons=dict(sorted(reasons.items())),
                graded_fraction=_ratio(success + failure, len(pubs)),
                success_rate_among_graded=_ratio(success, success + failure),
                outcomes=outcomes,
            ),
            forward_window_coverage=dict(
                association_kind="forward_window_not_physical_impulse",
                window_count=window_count,
                covered_count=len(covered),
                uncovered_count=len(uncovered),
                uncovered_forward_window_rate=_ratio(len(uncovered), window_count),
                conditional_catalog_coverage_rate=_ratio(len(covered), window_count),
                left_censored_window_count=sum(
                    w["left_censored"] for w in covered + uncovered
                ),
                alerts_without_forward_window_count=len(unmatched),
                alerts_without_forward_window=unmatched,
                covered_windows=covered,
                uncovered_windows=uncovered,
                physical_missed_move_rate=None,
            ),
        )
    return dict(
        **provenance(),
        knowledge_asof_ts_ms=asof_ts_ms,
        target_contract_sha256=contract_sha256,
        target_evaluator_sha256=evaluator_sha256,
        label_count=len(rows),
        alert_count=len(pubs),
        failed_alerts_that_cover_a_forward_window=forgiven,
        association_alters_primary_outcomes=False,
        horizons=tables,
    )


def exposure_report(labels, eligibility, *, asof_ts_ms, ranker_slots=None):
    """Causal decision-eligible exposure, separately from retrospective gradability."""
    _integer(asof_ts_ms)
    rows, contract_sha256, evaluator_sha256 = _labels(labels, asof_ts_ms)
    slots = {}
    for record in eligibility:
        if (
            not isinstance(record, dict)
            or "product_id" not in record
            or "ts_ms" not in record
            or any(type(record.get(flag)) is not bool for flag in ELIGIBILITY_FLAGS)
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "explicit boolean eligibility flags required; never coerce"
            )
        key = _text(record["product_id"]), _grid(record["ts_ms"])
        if key[1] > asof_ts_ms:
            raise ValueError("eligibility after the report as-of is not known")
        if key in slots:
            raise ValueError("duplicate or overlapping eligibility slot")
        slots[key] = {flag: record[flag] for flag in ELIGIBILITY_FLAGS}
    universe = Counter(ts for (_, ts), f in slots.items() if f["universe_member"])
    cutoffs = sorted({ts for _, ts in slots})
    sizes = [[ts, universe.get(ts, 0)] for ts in cutoffs]
    eligible, exclusions = set(), Counter()
    for key, flags in slots.items():
        failed = next((flag for flag in ELIGIBILITY_FLAGS if not flags[flag]), None)
        if failed is None:
            eligible.add(key)
        else:
            exclusions[failed] += 1
    rankers = {}
    for name, scored in (ranker_slots or {}).items():
        records = [(_text(p), _grid(ts)) for p, ts in scored]
        scored = set(records)
        if len(scored) != len(records):
            raise ValueError("duplicate ranker slot")
        if not scored <= set(slots):
            raise ValueError("ranker slots must be recorded eligibility slots")
        rankers[_text(name)] = scored
    horizons = {}
    for horizon in HORIZONS:
        gradable, reasons = set(), Counter()
        for product, ts in sorted(eligible):
            reason = None
            if ts + horizon * 1000 > asof_ts_ms:
                reason = "immature_trailing_horizon"
            for direction in DIRECTIONS if reason is None else ():
                label = rows.get((product, direction, horizon, ts))
                if label is None:
                    reason = f"label_missing:{direction}"
                elif label["status"] != "ready":
                    reason = f"{label['status']}:{direction}:{label.get('reason')}"
                if reason:
                    break
            if reason:
                reasons[reason] += 1
            else:
                gradable.add((product, ts))
        shared = set.intersection(*rankers.values()) if rankers else set(eligible)
        horizons[str(horizon)] = dict(
            horizon_seconds=horizon,
            gradable_slot_count=len(gradable),
            gradable_product_hours=_hours(len(gradable)),
            ungradable_eligible_slot_count=len(eligible) - len(gradable),
            ungradable_reasons=dict(sorted(reasons.items())),
            gradable_fraction_of_eligible=_ratio(len(gradable), len(eligible)),
            comparison=dict(
                identical_complete_row_count=len(shared & gradable),
                identical_complete_rows_definition=(
                    "decision_eligible_and_gradable_and_scored_by_every_ranker"
                ),
                natural_ranker_coverage={
                    name: dict(
                        eligible_scored_slot_count=len(scored & eligible),
                        gradable_scored_slot_count=len(scored & gradable),
                    )
                    for name, scored in sorted(rankers.items())
                },
            ),
        )
    return dict(
        **provenance(),
        knowledge_asof_ts_ms=asof_ts_ms,
        target_contract_sha256=contract_sha256,
        target_evaluator_sha256=evaluator_sha256,
        recorded_slot_count=len(slots),
        decision_eligible_slot_count=len(eligible),
        decision_eligible_product_hours=_hours(len(eligible)),
        board_wall_clock_hours_with_any_eligible_product=_hours(
            len({ts for _, ts in eligible})
        ),
        board_wall_clock_hours_with_any_verified_health=_hours(
            len({ts for (_, ts), f in slots.items() if f["health_verified"]})
        ),
        exclusions_by_first_failed_flag=dict(
            (flag, exclusions[flag]) for flag in ELIGIBILITY_FLAGS
        ),
        universe_size_by_cutoff=sizes,
        universe_size_first=sizes[0][1] if sizes else None,
        universe_size_last=sizes[-1][1] if sizes else None,
        universe_size_min=min((s for _, s in sizes), default=None),
        universe_size_max=max((s for _, s in sizes), default=None),
        decision_universe_independent_of_future_labels=True,
        horizons=horizons,
    )


def report(labels, alerts, eligibility, *, asof_ts_ms, ranker_slots=None):
    labels = list(labels)
    return dict(
        **provenance(),
        knowledge_asof_ts_ms=asof_ts_ms,
        association=association_report(labels, alerts, asof_ts_ms=asof_ts_ms),
        exposure=exposure_report(
            labels, eligibility, asof_ts_ms=asof_ts_ms, ranker_slots=ranker_slots
        ),
    )
